# Contracts

What our L2 contracts commit to, for a verifier that reads them from the chain and the archive alone. So far this page specifies the event accumulator every Abolish contract keeps, the contract-event record (`0x0008`) that archives each of its events, the election registry, the trustee registry, the group registry with its Tier 0 additions (P3-2) and its Tier 1 additions (P3-3), the Tier 2 scope's nullifier keys in the group registry (P3-4), the board, our direct-submit contract, with its ballot and delivery checks, calls and events, its relayed-ballot call, the close statements' contract path and the window it holds ordinary ballots to, and our three contracts on L1: the board anchor (P4-1), the relay that carries forced ballots to the board (P1-18 parts 4c-2b and 4c-3; the rule itself is [[close]]) and the release registry that ballot-client releases are published to (P4-4). The group registry's Tier 2 additions (P3-4) are added here as they are built. Notation is from [[notation]], tags from [[domain-separation]].

## Event accumulator

Threats: T-71 (L2 governance or our server hiding an event), T-25 (a direct-submitted ballot withheld from the board), T-24 (equivocation). Decided by [[0005-permanent-archive]], Consequences, "L2 events".

Our server writes the board, and inclusion proofs show only that an event exists, never that none was left out. So every Abolish contract keeps a running hash and a counter over every log it emits, in its own storage, and each step also binds the block that emitted the log. Storage is committed by the L2 state root, which L1 output roots commit to, so an account and storage proof (`eth_getProof`) of these two values at an L2 block pins the contract's whole event sequence up to that block, each event with its block number and timestamp. The block is always the emitting chain's: the board anchor and the relay, our contracts on L1, bind L1 blocks, and their evidence is L1's own state (Board anchor, L1 relay). A verifier replays the archived sequence and compares ([[verifier]] 4.4, P1-19). This is the only evidence archived for our contracts' events: no transaction, receipt or block header ([[0025-contract-event-evidence]]).

The tag is `abolish/v1/event-accumulator-2`. The first layout, under `abolish/v1/event-accumulator`, bound no block; it stays frozen with its vectors (`vectors/event-accumulator.json`) and no Abolish contract is deployed with it ([[0025-contract-event-evidence]], Consequences).

### Values

- **Genesis.** At deployment the contract stores

  ```
  acc_0 = H(DS("abolish/v1/event-accumulator-2", u8(0) ‖ u64(chain_id) ‖ bytes[20](address)))
  ```

  and a count of 0, where `chain_id` is the chain's EIP-155 identifier (`block.chainid`) and `address` the contract's own address. Binding both means one contract's event sequence can't be presented as another's, here or on another chain where the same address is deployed (T-71). It doesn't separate the two sides of a fork after deployment, which share the stored value: that is the job of the pinned L2 and the output root the storage proof is checked against (Verification). A contract MUST revert its deployment if `chain_id` is 2⁶⁴ or more, since it has no `u64` encoding.

- **Step.** For its `n`-th log (0-based), emitted in the block with number `b` and timestamp `τ` (`block.number` and `block.timestamp`, read in the call that emits it), with topics `t_0 … t_{k−1}` and data `d`, the contract sets

  ```
  acc_{n+1} = H(DS("abolish/v1/event-accumulator-2",
                   u8(1) ‖ acc_n ‖ u64(n) ‖ u64(b) ‖ u64(τ) ‖
                   u8(k) ‖ t_0 ‖ … ‖ t_{k−1} ‖ u32(len(d)) ‖ d))
  ```

  and the count to `n + 1`. A contract reverts the emitting call if `b` or `τ` is 2⁶⁴ or more, since it has no `u64` encoding. Each topic is the log's 32-byte topic as emitted, and `d` its data bytes as emitted (the ABI encoding of the event's non-indexed fields). `k` is 1 to 4. A log with no topics (a Solidity anonymous event) is never emitted, so `t_0` is always the event's signature hash.

The leading `u8` separates the two input layouts, and fixed widths plus the length of `d` make each input parse one way. The index `n` is redundant with the chain but makes each step's position explicit, so a verifier can report which event diverged. `b` and `τ` place each event on L2 for the checks that compare it with a block or a time ([[verifier]] 2.4, 5.5), with no header or receipt: the accumulator authenticates them with the rest of the step.

### Rules for every contract

- Every log a contract emits goes through the accumulator, in the same internal call that emits it, with no external call in between. No contract emits an anonymous event or any log the accumulator doesn't cover. A reverted call reverts both its logs and its accumulator update, so the two can't drift apart.
- The two values live at fixed storage slots, independent of the contract's inheritance, so a verifier can request them by slot. The base slot is the [ERC-7201](https://eips.ethereum.org/EIPS/eip-7201) namespace `abolish.event-accumulator`:

  ```
  S = keccak256(abi.encode(uint256(keccak256("abolish.event-accumulator")) − 1)) & ~0xff
    = 0x1073a090f5464cb55b258e445c2f90eb0f9b00fb8b1700430d20b9c53b939600
  ```

  The accumulator is the 32-byte word at `S`. The count is the word at `S + 1`, as a big-endian 256-bit integer whose top 192 bits are zero; a proven word with any of those bits set fails the check. ERC-7201's keccak derivation is an external format ([[domain-separation]]), not a protocol hash.

- `eventAccumulator()` returns `(bytes32 accumulator, uint64 count)` for convenience. A verifier relies on the storage proof, never on a call answered by an RPC node.

### Verification

Given a contract's address, the chain ID, its archived logs in order of `n`, each with its `b` and `τ`, and a storage proof of `S` and `S + 1` at an L2 block `B`, a verifier recomputes `acc` and the count from genesis over the logs. The archive matches the contract at `B` exactly when, for the proven count `c`:

1. the logs are numbered `0, 1, …` with no gap, and `b` and `τ` never decrease from one log to the next;
2. every log with `n < c` has `b ≤ B`, and the log `n = c`, if the archive holds one, has `b > B`;
3. the accumulator over logs `0 … c − 1` equals the proven value.

The rules are checked in that order, and the first that fails is reported: `index` (a log out of sequence: rule 1's numbering, checked over the whole archive first), `order` (a block number or timestamp that decreases, checked only once every log is numbered in sequence), `block-range` (rule 2) or `accumulator` (rule 3, including an archive holding fewer than `c` logs).

Anything else (a log left out, added, altered or reordered, a log from another contract or chain, or a log moved to another block) gives a different accumulator or breaks rule 1 or 2, except with negligible probability under SHA-256's collision resistance. The proof at `B` is of the state after `B`, so it covers `B`'s own logs; rule 2 keeps a log from being counted on the wrong side of `B` without changing the accumulator, and rule 1 holds for every honest history, since L2 block numbers and timestamps never decrease. This assumes the genesis and count the release constructor writes; until [[verifier]] 1.2 binds each deployment to the release creation code (Board anchor, Known-weak, Constructor effects aren't bound), a deployer's own initcode could start the accumulator elsewhere. The storage proof is an account proof for the contract's address as the verifier pins it ([[verifier]], Pinned trust roots; its bytecode is checked by 1.2) under the state root that the output root commits to, plus a storage proof of each slot. The storage trie holds each value as the RLP of its minimal big-endian bytes, and omits a zero value: the verifier decodes the value, left-pads it to 32 bytes, and reads an absent slot as zero. The accumulator commits to each log's order and block, never to its transaction: nothing about the transaction is archived ([[0025-contract-event-evidence]]). [[verifier]] 4.4 says when this is checked and what the result is worth: a storage proof is only as good as the output root it is proven against ([[0004-l2-choice]], T-71 residual).

### Output root

A reader holding an output root from L1 proves `S` and `S + 1` under it, so the L2 node that serves the proofs is trusted for nothing (T-71, T-34). The root is version 0 of the OP Stack's L2 output commitment (OP Stack specification, Proposals, L2 Output Commitment Construction), which Base's dispute games claim:

`output_root = keccak256(version ‖ state_root ‖ message_passer_storage_root ‖ block_hash)`

with `version` 32 zero bytes, `state_root` the L2 block's state root, `message_passer_storage_root` the storage root of the L2ToL1MessagePasser predeploy (`0x4200000000000000000000000000000000000016`) after the block, and `block_hash` the block's hash, each 32 bytes. The reader is given the root, the message passer's storage root, the block's RLP header and trie nodes (as `eth_getProof` returns them), and:

1. decodes the header as one canonical RLP list of at least the 15 fields every fork has, each a byte string, else `l2-header`;
2. recomputes the root from the header's state root, the given message-passer root and the Keccak-256 of the header's bytes, and refuses any other root (`output-root`), so the header, and with it `B` (its number) and its timestamp, is the one the root commits to;
3. proves the contract's account under the header's state root: an account that is absent, or whose code hash is that of empty code, is `l2-contract-absent`; a node the walk needs and doesn't have is `l2-proof-missing`, and a node, account or value that isn't well formed is `l2-proof-malformed`;
4. proves each slot under the account's storage root, as Verification says (an absent slot is zero), and refuses a count with any of its top 192 bits set (`l2-count`), which no contract writes.

The game that carries the root, and so how much the root is worth, is the reader's to choose and report ([[verifier]] 4.4): a root no game claims proves nothing, and an unresolved game's root is its proposer's word ([[0004-l2-choice]], T-71). So a reader takes roots only from games of the type the L2's portal respects (its `respectedGameType()`, from the factory its `disputeGameFactory()` names), whose proposals carry a TEE attestation or a ZK proof on Base, never of a type a reader configures: a type anyone can create without a proof would let anyone post a root, and with a colluding L2 node, prove a false accumulator. `vectors/output-root.json` gives a Base Sepolia game's root with the block and proofs it commits to, and inputs failing `output-root`, `l2-header`, `l2-proof-missing` and `l2-contract-absent`; the other codes need a state no honest node serves, so `packages/verifier`'s tests build one.

### Vectors

`vectors/event-accumulator-2.json` (format `abolish-event-accumulator-vectors/2`, see [[vectors/README|vectors]]) gives genesis values, event sequences with each event's block and the accumulator after every step, proof checks, including histories that break each rule of Verification, and rejected inputs. TypeScript (`packages/crypto`) and Solidity (`packages/contracts`) tests both consume it. `vectors/event-accumulator.json` is the frozen first layout's, consumed by nothing.

### Known-weak

- The accumulator proves that the archive matches the contract's storage, not that the storage is honest: L2 governance that rewrites state can rewrite it too. A verifier that derives the L2 from L1 itself while the data is retrievable detects it; afterwards only the output root vouches for the storage, as for everything else on L2 (T-71, [[0004-l2-choice]], Finality; [[0025-contract-event-evidence]], Known-weak).
- Each event costs one SHA-256 precompile call over its log, plus two storage writes: 60 gas plus 12 per 32-byte word for the hash; binding `b` and `τ` adds at most one word. Voters don't transact to vote, so it is mostly our gas, but a direct submitter (T-25) pays it for their own submission.

## Contract-event record

Threats: T-17 (chosen bytes in the archive), T-31 (two readings of one record), T-71 (an event the chain's history doesn't hold), T-25 (a direct-submitted event withheld from the board), T-52 (oversized input), T-53 (a submitter failing an election it can't win). Decided by [[0025-contract-event-evidence]] (option B).

Every event of every one of our L2 contracts is archived, and the ones an election's checks use are boarded ([[board]], Payloads; [[verifier]] 4.4), as record type `0x0008`: exactly what its contract's accumulator step hashes, nothing more. A record says which contract emitted the log, its place in that contract's sequence, its L2 block and the log itself; the accumulator's storage proofs authenticate all of it at once (Event accumulator, Verification), so no transaction, receipt or block header is archived, and no byte the sender chose outside our events' own fields reaches the archive (T-17).

### Layout (versions 1 to 13)

| Field          | Type                    | Meaning                                                                             |
| -------------- | ----------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `contract`     | `bytes[20]`             | The emitting contract's address on the definition's L2 (`l2_chain_id`)              |
| `n`            | `u64`                   | The log's index in that contract's event sequence, from 0 (Event accumulator, Step) |
| `l2_block`     | `u64`                   | `b`: the number of the L2 block that emitted it                                     |
| `l2_timestamp` | `u64`                   | `τ`: that block's timestamp                                                         |
| `topics`       | `list<bytes[32], 4>`    | The log's topics as emitted, `topics[0]` its event's signature hash                 |
| `data`         | `bytes<MAX_EVENT_DATA>` | The log's data as emitted: the ABI encoding of the event's non-indexed fields       |

`MAX_EVENT_DATA` is 2²⁰ (1,048,576) bytes, a framing bound checked before anything is allocated (T-52); every event of the tables below is far smaller: under version 1 the largest, a `MembersAdded`, is at most 25,120 bytes; under versions 2 and 3 a `CeremonyRegistered` at most 41,472, a `DefinitionRegistered` about 66 KB, and a `BallotSubmitted` at most about 175 KB as framed (the most 32 questions of 65 branches can frame; an admitted ballot's shape bounds it far lower), a `RelayedBallotSubmitted` 128 bytes more; under version 9 a `BallotSubmitted` carrying ballot version 4 at most about 209 KB as framed (version 3's most, and an author half of `MAX_OPTIONS` rows of `MAX_TRUSTEES` elements with `MAX_TRUSTEES` share hashes) and an `AuthorPanelRegistered` at most 41,472 as framed, as `CeremonyRegistered`, which has its layout (2,784 bytes of data for the 2,690-byte record of 16 seats the registry allows); under version 10 a `RollingBallotSubmitted` carrying ballot version 5 at most about 209 KB as framed, version 4's most with its 4-byte step and the supplied root's word; under version 11 a `RelayedRollingBallotSubmitted` 128 bytes more, its four call fields; under version 12 a `PanelPinned` 32 bytes more than version 1's, its kind's word; under version 13 a `DefinitionRegistered` carrying a version-7 definition at most about 113 KB as framed, version 6's most with the mode and a `rolling_electorate` of three tiers of 1,024 groups each (about 25 KB; decoding doesn't empty either electorate, which is well-formedness's). An event that needs more is a new record version.

The fields are the step's input, re-framed in [[notation]]'s primitives: the step counts topics with a `u8`, the record with a `list`'s `u32`, so a third-party decoder needs no construct of its own, and the step is recomputed from the decoded values, `k` being the number of topics. Decoding is strict ([[notation]], Strict decoding) and context-free; a record with no topics, or with topics or data that aren't an event of its contract, decodes and then fails the event check below. A record's `chain_id` is the definition's `l2_chain_id`, never a field.

Our contracts on L1 have no contract-event records: a verifier reads the board anchor's anchors and latest locator, and the relay's recorded calls, from L1 state, never from their logs (Board anchor, Verification; L1 relay, Verification). The one exception is the anchor's earlier locators, which only its `ArchiveLocated` logs keep: a verifier takes them from the anchor's whole log sequence, replayed against the accumulator in the anchor's L1 state (Archive locator; [[verifier]], Archive), never from a list of logs alone.

Versions 2 to 13 have version 1's layout, field for field; the thirteen differ only in their event tables and in what the board's framing reads (Event check). The election's profile pins one version for every contract's events, and a record of another is `profile-mismatch` ([[versioning]], Election profile).

### Event check

A record that decodes counts only if it passes these checks, in order, against the definition's L2 contract addresses and the event tables of the record's version; the first that fails is the result, and [[verifier]] 4.4 fails, naming the record:

1. `unknown-contract`: `contract` is the definition's `election_registry`, `trustee_registry`, `group_registry` or `board`, which the `chain` rule keeps pairwise distinct, giving the record's **role**. Any other address, the L1 anchor's included, fails.
2. `unknown-event`: the record has at least one topic and `topics[0]` is the keccak-256 of an event signature in its role's table for the version (below). An anonymous log, or another role's event, fails.
3. `event-topics`: the record has exactly the event's topics, one per indexed parameter after `topics[0]`, and each is the canonical ABI word of its type: an `address` or `uint<M>` word has no bit set above its width (a `bytes32` word is any 32 bytes).
4. `event-data`: `data` is exactly the canonical ABI encoding of the event's non-indexed parameters (the [Solidity ABI specification](https://docs.soliditylang.org/en/v0.8.30/abi-spec.html), as `abi.encode` produces it): one head word per parameter, a static value's word canonical as in check 3, each dynamic value's offset word pointing exactly past the head and the dynamic values before it, each dynamic value a length word (below 2⁶⁴) and that many bytes, or words for an array, padded with zero bytes to a whole word, and nothing after the last. Each embedded value also meets the bound and the stateless checks of its contract, as the table says.

So each event has exactly one record: the data and topics of every passing record are exactly those re-encoded from its decoded event (the property tests rebuild both), and none carries a byte its event doesn't define (T-17, T-31). The check is of the record's form only. Whether the log is the contract's own is the accumulator's to show (Event accumulator, Verification, over the contract's records in order of `n`); what each field means, and whether it agrees with the others (a definition's hash with its bytes, a group record's identifier with its topic), is for the checks that use the event ([[verifier]] 2.1, 2.4, 2.5, 3.1).

**Event tables (version 1).** Each role's events are its section's Events tables, read as ABI signatures:

| Role                | Event signature                                                     | Indexed                                 | Stateless checks on the data                                                                                                                                               |
| ------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `election_registry` | `ElectionCreated(bytes32,address,uint64,uint64,uint64,bytes32)`     | `electionId`, `creator`                 | none beyond the types (128 bytes)                                                                                                                                          |
| `election_registry` | `DefinitionRegistered(bytes32,bytes32,uint64,uint64,bytes32,bytes)` | `electionId`, `definitionHash`          | `definition` decodes as election-definition version 1 with its self-pin ([[election-definition]], Decoding), the registry's rule 2; well-formedness and the hash are 2.1's |
| `trustee_registry`  | `PanelPinned(bytes32,uint8,uint8,bytes)`                            | `panelId`                               | `keys` is 1 to `MAX_TRUSTEES` whole 33-byte encodings, each a `p256-key` ([[trustee-identity]], Identity key)                                                              |
| `trustee_registry`  | `CeremonyRegistered(bytes32,bytes32,bytes32,bytes)`                 | `stubHash`, `panelId`, `transcriptHash` | `registration` is at least 97 bytes and exactly `97 + 65 · count`, the first registration layout, without the key set (Known-weak)                                         |
| `group_registry`    | `RegistrarsPinned(uint8,address[])`                                 | none                                    | at most `MAX_REGISTRARS` addresses                                                                                                                                         |
| `group_registry`    | `GroupCreated(uint64,uint8)`                                        | `groupId`, `tier`                       | no data                                                                                                                                                                    |
| `group_registry`    | `MembersAdded(uint64,uint64,bytes)`                                 | `groupId`, `firstIndex`                 | `record` passes the addition record's stateless checks: length, batch and forbidden commitments (Group registry, Additions)                                                |
| `group_registry`    | `MembersRemoved(uint64,bytes)`                                      | `groupId`                               | `record` passes the removal record's stateless checks: length, batch and index order and range (Group registry, Removals)                                                  |
| `board`             | none in version 1                                                   |                                         | Every version-1 record naming the board fails `unknown-event`; its events (Board, Events) come in this record's version 2 (Event tables (version 2), below)                |

Every log the release contracts emit passes, since each emits only through its own ABI encoder, after the same stateless checks; `packages/contracts` compares each registry's compiled ABI with this table. The tables are part of version 1 and freeze with its vectors ([[versioning]], Change rules): a record that fails under version 1 fails under it for good. So the board's events, and any event a later contract adds, come in a new version of `0x0008` whose tables extend these, and a profile that pins it reads every contract's events under it; a profile pinning version 1 can name no board whose events count, which is why direct submission brings version 2 with it.

**Event tables (version 2).** Version 1's rows, with the registries' two changed rows read as the deployable registries emit them, and the board's events (Board, Events):

| Role                | Event signature                                                     | Indexed                                 | Stateless checks on the data                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| ------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `election_registry` | `ElectionCreated(bytes32,address,uint64,uint64,uint64,bytes32)`     | `electionId`, `creator`                 | As version 1                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `election_registry` | `DefinitionRegistered(bytes32,bytes32,uint64,uint64,bytes32,bytes)` | `electionId`, `definitionHash`          | `definition` decodes under the layout its version byte names, version 1 or 3 to 6, with its self-pin ([[election-definition]], Decoding), the registry's rule 2. Any other version fails, version 7 and every later one included, since this registry can't emit it; well-formedness and the hash are 2.1's                                                    |
| `trustee_registry`  | `PanelPinned(bytes32,uint8,uint8,bytes)`                            | `panelId`                               | As version 1                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `trustee_registry`  | `CeremonyRegistered(bytes32,bytes32,bytes32,bytes)`                 | `stubHash`, `panelId`, `transcriptHash` | `registration` has the key-set layout (Trustee registry, Registration, check 2): at least the 97-byte header, at least `98 + 97 · seats` bytes with `seats` at byte 96, and exactly `98 + 97 · seats + 65 · count` with `count` at byte `97 + 97 · seats`. The seats' order and points, the approvals and the quorum are the registry's and [[verifier]] 3.1's |
| `group_registry`    | The four events of version 1                                        | As version 1                            | As version 1                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `board`             | `BallotSubmitted(bytes32,bytes32,bytes)`                            | `definitionHash`, `ballotHash`          | `record` is a ballot record the board frames (below), and the topics are its `definition_hash` and its `ballot_hash`, recomputed from its bytes (Board, Ballots, step 9)                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `board`             | `ShareReceiptSubmitted(bytes32,bytes32,bytes)`                      | `definitionHash`, `ballotHash`          | `record` is a share receipt (`0x000c`) the board frames (below), and the topics are its `definition_hash` and `ballot_hash`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| `board`             | `SharePosted(bytes32,bytes32,bytes)`                                | `definitionHash`, `ballotHash`          | `record` is a share posting (`0x000d`) the board frames, and the topics are its `definition_hash` and `ballot_hash`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `board`             | `ShareComplaintSubmitted(bytes32,bytes32,bytes)`                    | `definitionHash`, `ballotHash`          | `record` is a share complaint (`0x000e`) the board frames, and the topics are its `definition_hash` and `ballot_hash`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |

**What the board frames.** The event check is context-free, so it runs the board's framing, which needs no election, and nothing more: the steps below. The board also refuses a few records without reading its registries (seat 0, a vote whose counts or degrees disagree with each other, a posting whose `sealed` length fits no option count), which the event check leaves to the verifier, as it does every shape check: each check added here would be one more way a record the board emits could fail it (T-53), and the accumulator already shows that every archived record is a log the board emitted.

- A ballot record: record type `0x0002` and a version from 1 to 3 (step 1, and step 3 without the pin); step 4's fixed fields (version 1's `tier` at most 2, `nullifier` below `r`); step 5's committed vote, framed under the record's own version, with nothing after it; and the membership proof's three points canonical and each in its group, as [[membership]] (Proof encoding) decodes them, `B`'s order-`r` subgroup included, which the board's `ecPairing` checks (Membership proofs). Whether the version is the one the election pins, and steps 6 to 9 but the proof's points, read the registries, so the board judged them when it accepted the record and the verifier judges them again ([[verifier]] 5).
- A delivery record: its call's type, version 1, and step 4's framing: a receipt of exactly 164 bytes with a `p256-sig` whose `r` and `s` are in range, a posting with its `ct_dh` framed, `pok_c` and `pok_z` below `ℓ` and `sealed` at most 4,116 bytes, a complaint of exactly 228 bytes with its `D` framed, `c` and `z` below `ℓ` and a `p256-sig` in range. The seat, the signature's verification and a posting's length and ballot read the registries.

An element is framed as the board frames it, never decoded: an even value `s` with `0 < s < 2²⁵⁵ − 19`, read little-endian. The board accepts a member's ballot whose elements frame but aren't ristretto255 points, which the verifier rejects later as an entry that counts nothing (Board, Ballots, What it leaves out); if the event check decoded them, that member could fail every election through the board's shared sequence with one ballot ([[verifier]] 4.4; T-53). The ballot hash is recomputed from the record's bytes, `H(DS("abolish/v1/ballot", record[3:35] ‖ record[0:3] ‖ record[35:68] ‖ record[364:]))`, which is [[ballot-validity]]'s (Ballot hash; Hashes (version 3)) for versions 1 to 3, so a topic that disagrees with its record's bytes fails `event-data` and no record has two readings (T-31). Every log the board emits passes, since it emits a record only after its own check, which runs all of these.

Version 2 freezes with its vectors as version 1 did, and every event a later contract adds comes in a later version: the close rule's two board events come in version 3 (below). So does every record a later board frames: version 2's framing reads ballot versions 1 to 3 and delivery version 1 only, and a board's sequence is shared by every election on it, so a board that accepts ballot version 4 or a version-2 delivery record ([[delivery]], Version 2; P2-16 part 5) MUST come with a contract-event version whose framing reads them, and every profile naming that board MUST pin that version, or each election pinning version 2 fails 4.4 at the board's first such event (T-53). A profile that pins version 2 reads every contract's events under these tables, the registries' included; version 1's `CeremonyRegistered` check refuses every record the current trustee registry emits, and its tables name no board event, so version 1 MUST be retired before any registry or board is deployed, and the election registry refuses a profile pinning it (Election registry, Registration, rule 9). [[verifier]] 2.3 fails one too (`profile-combination`, [[close]], Version), so an election a registry that doesn't refuse it registers can't verify.

**Event tables (version 3).** Version 2's rows, every one unchanged, and the board's two events of the close rule ([[0030-close-rule]]; Board, Relayed ballots and Close statements):

| Role       | Event signature                                                             | Indexed                        | Stateless checks on the data                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| ---------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------ | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| every role | The events of version 2                                                     | As version 2                   | As version 2                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `board`    | `RelayedBallotSubmitted(bytes32,bytes32,uint64,uint64,uint64,uint64,bytes)` | `definitionHash`, `ballotHash` | `index`, `l1Block`, `stamp` and `gasLimit` are canonical `uint64` words (check 3); `record` is a ballot record the board frames (What the board frames), and the topics are its `definition_hash` and its `ballot_hash`, recomputed from its bytes, as for `BallotSubmitted`. Whether the call fields match a relay call is 4.5's                                 |
| `board`    | `CloseStatementSubmitted(bytes32,uint8,bytes)`                              | `definitionHash`, `signer`     | `record` decodes strictly as a close statement of version 1 ([[close]], Close statement, Decoding: 180 bytes, a canonical low-`s` `p256-sig`), and the topics are its `definition_hash` and its `signer`, the signer's word canonical as a `uint8` (check 3, else `event-topics`). Its seat, signature, `L_δ` and time are the verifier's ([[close]], Close head) |

Version 3 fixes the close statement's version on the contract path at 1, as board-entry versions 5 to 7 fix it on the board path ([[close]], Version): a statement of any other version fails `event-data`. A relayed ballot is framed exactly as an ordinary one, so version 3's framing, like version 2's, reads ballot versions 1 to 3 and delivery version 1 only; a board that accepts ballot version 4 or a version-2 delivery record still needs a later version (above). Version 3 freezes with its vectors.

A board with the relayed-ballot call or the statement call emits these events into the sequence every election on it shares, and version 2 names neither: each election pinning version 2 would fail 4.4 at the first one, whatever election it is for (T-53). So version 2 MUST be retired, as version 1 must, before any board with these calls is deployed, and the election registry MUST refuse a profile pinning either (Election registry, Registration). [[verifier]] 2.3 fails such a profile (`profile-combination`, [[close]], Version).

**Event tables (version 4).** Version 3's rows, every one unchanged, and the board's two batch events ([[P1-12d]], [[P1-12e]]; Board, Delivery records and Events):

| Role       | Event signature                                     | Indexed                  | Stateless checks on the data                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| ---------- | --------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------ | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| every role | The events of version 3                             | As version 3             | As version 3                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `board`    | `ShareComplaintBatchSubmitted(bytes32,uint8,bytes)` | `definitionHash`, `seat` | `record` is a share-complaint batch of version 1 the board frames ([[delivery]], Share-complaint batch: 1 to 512 entries, strictly ascending, each `D` framed as an element is framed, never decoded, `c` and `z` below `ℓ`, a canonical low-`s` `p256-sig`, nothing after), and the topics are its `definition_hash` and its `seat`, the seat's word canonical as a `uint8`. Whether each `D` is a point, and the signature and proofs, are the delivery check's |
| `board`    | `ShareReceiptBatchSubmitted(bytes32,uint8,bytes)`   | `definitionHash`, `seat` | `record` decodes strictly as a share-receipt batch of version 1 ([[delivery]], Share-receipt batch, Decoding: 1 to 1,024 pairs, strictly ascending, a canonical low-`s` `p256-sig`, nothing after), and the topics are its `definition_hash` and its `seat`, the seat's word canonical as a `uint8` (check 3, else `event-topics`). Its seat, signature and pairs are the delivery check's                                                                        |

Version 4 fixes both batches' versions on the contract path at 1, the version ballot versions 1 to 3 fix ([[delivery]], Versions): a batch of any other version fails `event-data`. A complaint batch is framed, never decoded, as the board frames a single complaint, so a seat's batch with a `D` that isn't a point is emitted and passes, and the delivery check rejects it (T-53). Its framing of every other record is version 3's, so it reads ballot versions 1 to 3 and delivery version 1 only; a board that accepts ballot version 4 or a version-2 delivery record still needs a later version (above). Version 4 freezes with its vectors.

A board with the batch call emits its event into the sequence every election on it shares, and version 3 doesn't name it: each election pinning version 3 would fail 4.4 at the first batch, whatever election it is for (T-53). So version 3 MUST be retired, as versions 1 and 2 must, before any board with the batch call is deployed, and the election registry MUST refuse a profile pinning any of them (Election registry, Registration, rule 9). [[verifier]] 2.3 fails such a profile (`profile-combination`, [[close]], Version).

**Event tables (version 5).** Version 4's rows, every one unchanged, and the board's obligation-root event ([[P1-12f]]; Board, Delivery records and Events):

| Role       | Event signature                                | Indexed                  | Stateless checks on the data                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| ---------- | ---------------------------------------------- | ------------------------ | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| every role | The events of version 4                        | As version 4             | As version 4                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `board`    | `ObligationRootSubmitted(bytes32,uint8,bytes)` | `definitionHash`, `seat` | `record` decodes strictly as an obligation root of version 1 ([[delivery]], Obligation root, Decoding: 173 bytes, `kind` 1 or 2, a non-zero `count`, a `leaves_cid` framed as its kind and count give, a canonical low-`s` `p256-sig`, nothing after), and the topics are its `definition_hash` and its `seat`, the seat's word canonical as a `uint8` (check 3, else `event-topics`). Its seat, signature, slot, count and leaves are the delivery check's |

Version 5 fixes the obligation root's version on the contract path at 1, the version ballot versions 1 to 3 fix ([[delivery]], Versions): a root of any other version fails `event-data`. A root has no element to frame, and its CID's framing follows from fields it carries, so the board's check and the event check decode it alike. Its framing of every other record is version 4's, so it reads ballot versions 1 to 3 and delivery version 1 only; a board that accepts ballot version 4 or a version-2 delivery record still needs a later version (above). Version 5 freezes with its vectors.

A board with the root call emits its event into the sequence every election on it shares, and version 4 doesn't name it: each election pinning version 4 would fail 4.4 at the first root, whatever election it is for (T-53). So version 4 MUST be retired, as versions 1 to 3 must, before any board with the root call is deployed, and the election registry deployed with it MUST refuse a profile pinning any of them. The deployable registry pins version 5 (Election registry, Registration, rule 9) and [[verifier]] 2.3 requires it ([[P1-12f]] part 1b-2), so it refuses a profile pinning any of versions 1 to 4.

**Event tables (version 6).** Version 5's rows, every one unchanged, and the group registry's Tier 1 addition event ([[P3-3]]; Group registry, Tier 1 additions and Events):

| Role             | Event signature           | Indexed      | Stateless checks on the data                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| ---------------- | ------------------------- | ------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| every role       | The events of version 5   | As version 5 | As version 5                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `group_registry` | `Tier1MemberAdded(bytes)` | none         | `record` decodes strictly as a Tier 1 addition record ([[vouching]], Tier 1 addition record: exactly `41 + 626 · VOUCH_K` bytes with `VOUCH_K` at offset 40, `C_V`, each root and each nullifier below `r`, and each proof's three points canonical and in their groups, `B`'s order-`r` subgroup included). Whether it counts is [[vouching]]'s counting rule's |

The record carries no version byte: its layout is fixed by the registry deployment (Tier 1 additions), and version 6 reads the layout of `VOUCH_K = 3`. Every record the registry logs passes, since its rules 2, 4, 5 and 7 refuse every record that doesn't decode (rule 4 a `C_V` at or above `r`, rule 5 a root or nullifier, rule 7, through `MembershipProofLib`, a proof point that isn't canonical or in its group), so the check is never stricter than the registry (T-53). It is deliberately looser than the registry: a record the registry would refuse for a reason the decoder doesn't see (a group, a forbidden commitment, a slot, a root window, a duplicate, a proof that fails its pairing) passes the event check, and the counting rule refuses it as an addition that counts for nothing ([[vouching]], A failing addition never fails anything else). Version 6 freezes with its vectors.

A group registry with the Tier 1 call emits its event into the sequence every election shares, and version 5 doesn't name it: each election pinning version 5 would fail 4.4 at the first Tier 1 addition, whatever election it is for (T-53). So version 5 MUST be retired, as versions 1 to 4 must, before any group registry with the Tier 1 call is deployed, and the election registry deployed with it MUST refuse a profile pinning any of them, as [[verifier]] 2.3 must. The deployable registry pinned version 6 from [[P3-3]] part 3b-2a (Election registry, Registration, rule 9), and 2.3 required it, so it refused a profile pinning any of versions 1 to 5; both now pin version 8 (Event tables (version 8)); the counting rule reads the event (`groupRegistryEvents`, part 3b-2b-1), and no group registry may be deployed before P3-4 in any case (Group registry, Known-weak).

**Event tables (version 7).** Version 6's rows, every one unchanged, and the group registry's two self-paid events ([[P3-2]] part 5; Group registry, Self-paid additions and Events):

| Role             | Event signature                            | Indexed            | Stateless checks on the data                                                                                                                                          |
| ---------------- | ------------------------------------------ | ------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| every role       | The events of version 6                    | As version 6       | As version 6                                                                                                                                                          |
| `group_registry` | `SelfPaidGroupCreated(uint64)`             | `groupId`          | no data                                                                                                                                                               |
| `group_registry` | `SelfPaidMemberAdded(uint64,uint64,bytes)` | `groupId`, `index` | `record` passes the self-paid record's stateless checks: exactly 40 bytes, and a commitment that isn't forbidden (Group registry, Self-paid additions, rules 2 and 6) |

Every record the registry logs passes, since its rules 2 and 6 refuse every record that fails these checks, so the check is never stricter than the registry (T-53). Whether the group is self-paid, and whether `index` is the group's next one, are 2.4's to judge in its replay (Group registry, Verification), as for `MembersAdded`'s `first_index`. Version 7 freezes with its vectors.

A group registry with the self-paid calls emits both events into the sequence every election shares, and version 6 names neither, so each election pinning version 6 would fail 4.4 at the first self-paid group anyone creates (T-53). So version 6 MUST be retired, as versions 1 to 5 must, before any group registry with the self-paid calls is deployed, and the election registry deployed with it MUST refuse a profile pinning any of them, as [[verifier]] 2.3 must. `packages/contracts` builds the self-paid calls and version 7's vectors check the events they emit ([[P3-2]] part 5b-1). The deployable election registry pinned version 7 from [[P3-2]] part 5b-2 (Election registry, Registration, rule 9), and 2.3 required it, so it refused a profile pinning any of versions 1 to 6; both now pin version 8 (Event tables (version 8)). `packages/crypto` reads both events and replays self-paid groups ([[P3-2]] part 5c; Group registry, Verification); no group registry may be deployed before P3-4 in any case (Group registry, Known-weak).

**Event tables (version 8).** Version 7's rows, every one unchanged, and the board's two availability events ([[availability]], Contract path; [[P4-3]] part 2c-2; Board, Availability records and Events):

| Role       | Event signature                                         | Indexed                  | Stateless checks on the data                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| ---------- | ------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------ | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| every role | The events of version 7                                 | As version 7             | As version 7                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `board`    | `AvailabilityAttestationSubmitted(bytes32,uint8,bytes)` | `definitionHash`, `seat` | `record` decodes strictly as an availability attestation of version 1 ([[availability]], Decoding: `panel` and `point` 1 or 2, at most 32 roots, none at point 1, each above the one before, a canonical low-`s` `p256-sig`, nothing after: 138 to 1,162 bytes), and the topics are its `definition_hash` and its `seat`, the seat's word canonical as a `uint8` (check 3, else `event-topics`). Its panel, seat, signature, roots, checkpoint and time are [[availability]]'s check's |
| `board`    | `AvailabilityComplaintSubmitted(bytes32,uint8,bytes)`   | `definitionHash`, `seat` | `record` decodes strictly as an availability complaint of version 1 ([[availability]], Decoding: `panel` and `point` 1 or 2, a `cid` beginning `0x01 0x55 0x12 0x20` or `0x01 0x70 0x12 0x20`, a canonical low-`s` `p256-sig`, nothing after: 170 bytes), and the topics are its `definition_hash` and its `seat`, as for the attestation. Its panel, seat, signature, checkpoint and time are [[availability]]'s check's                                                              |

Version 8 fixes both records' versions on the contract path at 1 ([[availability]], Versions): a record of any other version fails `event-data`, and so does a record of the other type, since each event carries only its own. Neither record has an element to frame, so the board's check and the event check decode them alike. Its framing of every other record is version 7's, so it reads ballot versions 1 to 3 and delivery version 1 only; a board that accepts ballot version 4 or a version-2 delivery record still needs a later version (above). Version 8 freezes with its vectors.

A board with the availability call emits both events into the sequence every election on it shares, and versions 5 to 7 name neither: each election pinning one of them would fail 4.4 at the first attestation any seat sends, whatever election it is for (T-53). So versions 5 to 7 MUST be retired, as versions 1 to 4 must, before any board with the availability call is deployed, and the election registry deployed with it MUST refuse a profile pinning any of them, as [[verifier]] 2.3 must, which is also what [[availability]] (Contract path) requires of a profile whose version carries no availability event. `packages/contracts` builds the call and version 8's vectors check the events it emits ([[P4-3]] part 2c-2). The deployable election registry pins version 8 (Election registry, Registration, rule 9) and 2.3 requires it ([[P4-3]] part 2c-3), so it refuses a profile pinning any of versions 1 to 7. The verifier reads version 8's records in 4.4, ballot admission and the counted set, and the board's availability events in 4.6, for the ballot extent only so far ([[verifier]], Availability).

**Event tables (version 9).** Version 8's rows, every one unchanged but the board's eight rows that carry a ballot or a delivery record, which read them under version 9's framing (below), and the election registry's `AuthorPanelRegistered` ([[P2-16]] part 5; Election registry, Author panel registration and Events; Board, Ballots (version 4) and Delivery records (version 2)):

| Role                | Event signature                                                                                                                                        | Indexed                           | Stateless checks on the data                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| ------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | --------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| every role          | The events of version 8 not named below                                                                                                                | As version 8                      | As version 8                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `election_registry` | `AuthorPanelRegistered(bytes32,bytes32,bytes)`                                                                                                         | `definitionHash`, `authorPanelId` | `registration` has the trustee registry's key-set layout, as version 2's `CeremonyRegistered` (at least the 97-byte header, at least `98 + 97 · seats` bytes with `seats` at byte 96, and exactly `98 + 97 · seats + 65 · count` with `count` at byte `97 + 97 · seats`), and the `authorPanelId` topic is its first 32 bytes, its `panel_id`. The panel against the definition's author keys, the stub, the transcript, the seats' order and points, the approvals and the quorum are the registry's and [[verifier]] 3.1's, and so is the `definitionHash` topic against the definition |
| `board`             | `BallotSubmitted(bytes32,bytes32,bytes)`, `RelayedBallotSubmitted(bytes32,bytes32,uint64,uint64,uint64,uint64,bytes)`                                  | As version 8                      | As version 8, the ballot record framed under version 9 (ballot versions 1 to 4)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `board`             | `ShareReceiptSubmitted(bytes32,bytes32,bytes)`, `SharePosted(bytes32,bytes32,bytes)`, `ShareComplaintSubmitted(bytes32,bytes32,bytes)`                 | As version 8                      | As version 8, the record framed under version 9 (version 1 or 2)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `board`             | `ShareReceiptBatchSubmitted(bytes32,uint8,bytes)`, `ShareComplaintBatchSubmitted(bytes32,uint8,bytes)`, `ObligationRootSubmitted(bytes32,uint8,bytes)` | As version 8                      | `record` is a batch or root of version 1 or 2 read as version 9's framing reads it (below), and the topics are its `definition_hash` and its `seat`, the seat's word canonical as a `uint8` (check 3, else `event-topics`): the seat at offset 35 in a version-1 record and at 36 in a version-2 one, after its `panel`. A record of any other version fails `event-data`. Its panel, seat, signature, entries, slot and leaves are the delivery check's                                                                                                                                  |

**What the board frames (version 9).** Version 8's framing (What the board frames), with what a board that checks ballot version 4 and the version-2 delivery records accepts, and nothing more; still context-free, so it reads every version such a board accepts for any election, and leaves to the verifier whether a record's version is the one its election's ballot version fixes ([[delivery]], Versions; [[verifier]] 5, 5.9):

- A ballot record: record type `0x0002` and a version from 1 to 4. Versions 1 to 3 are framed as under version 8. A version-4 record is framed as Ballots (version 4) frames it without the registries: step 4's fixed fields as version 3's (`cell` any `u8`, `nullifier` below `r`), step 5's committed vote in its six fields, `author_vss` framed as `vss` and `author_share_hashes` as `share_hashes`, each count against its maximum before what it counts and each element framed as an element is framed (below), with nothing after it, and the membership proof's three points as under version 8. `validity-shape` and `share-hash-unqualified` read the registries' parameters and key sets, so the board judged them when it accepted the record and the verifier judges them again ([[ballot-validity]], Admission (version 4)). The ballot hash is recomputed from the record's bytes with the same formula as for versions 1 to 3, which is Hashes (version 4)'s, the version byte 4 among the bytes it hashes.
- A share receipt, posting or complaint: its call's type and version 1, framed as under version 8, or version 2, framed as version 1 with `panel` at offset 67, `1` or `2` ([[delivery]], Records (version 2), Decoding), and every later field one byte further on: a receipt of exactly 165 bytes, a complaint of exactly 229, a posting with its `ct_dh` framed, `pok_c` and `pok_z` below `ℓ` and `sealed` at most 4,116 bytes. The topics are its `definition_hash` and `ballot_hash` at offsets 3 and 35 under either version.
- A share-receipt batch or obligation root: version 1 decoded strictly as under versions 4 and 5, or version 2 decoded strictly ([[delivery]], Records (version 2), Decoding: `panel` 1 or 2 at 35, a receipt batch of 1 to 1,024 pairs strictly ascending, a root of 174 bytes with its `kind`, a non-zero `count` and a `leaves_cid` framed as its kind and count give, a canonical low-`s` `p256-sig`, nothing after). A share-complaint batch: version 1 framed as under version 4, or version 2 framed as version 1 with `panel` at 35 and `seat` at 36, each `D` framed, never decoded, as the board frames a single complaint's.

So every record a board deployed with ballot version 4 and the version-2 delivery records emits passes, since it emits a record only after its own check, which runs all of these, and the check is never stricter than the board (T-53): a member's version-4 ballot whose author element frames but isn't a point passes, as a version-3 one does, and so does an author seat's complaint batch with such a `D`; the verifier rejects both later. A version-2 record carries its panel inside its bytes, which the topics don't repeat, so a reader that finds an election's records by topic finds both panels' records of a seat index together and tells them apart by the record (T-37). Each event carries one record and every record has one reading: the version byte fixes the layout, the topics are recomputed from the bytes under it, so a version-2 batch whose `seat` topic is its panel byte, or a version-1 batch relabelled version 2, fails `event-data` (T-31). Version 9 frames neither ballot version 5 nor any later one: a board that accepts ballot version 5 ([[P2-17]]) needs a later version, as this one does for ballot version 4.

`AuthorPanelRegistered` is checked as `CeremonyRegistered` is, since its record is the trustee registry's layout ([[0035-author-key-set-on-l2]]), and in addition its `authorPanelId` topic must be the record's `panel_id`, so the event's two readings of the panel agree: the registry's check 2 makes the record's `panel_id` the definition's author panel identifier, which it emits as the topic, so every record it logs passes (T-53). The `definitionHash` topic needs the definition to judge, which the event check doesn't read, so [[verifier]] 3.1 for the author panel is to compare it, and the record's stub, transcript and key set, with the definition it registered after (Election registry, Events). 3.1 counts the registration's approvals beside the author approvals records since [[P2-16]] part 5d-3a, and is to fail an election whose recorded author key set hash isn't its transcript's once it reads it ([[ceremony]], Author panel, The registration's approvals), before rule 9 serves any definition that could emit it (part 5e). A record the registry would refuse for a reason the layout doesn't show (an approval short of the quorum, a seat out of order, a key that isn't a point) passes the event check and fails 3.1, as a `CeremonyRegistered` does; the registry emits none of them.

Version 9 reads every record version 8 passes exactly as version 8 does, with the same fields, and passes a record version 8 fails only if it carries one of the versions version 9 adds (a version-4 ballot, a version-2 receipt, posting, complaint, batch or root) or is an `AuthorPanelRegistered`; any other version-8 failure fails under version 9 with the same code, except that a record with `AuthorPanelRegistered`'s topic 0 that fails under version 9 (`event-topics` or `event-data`) fails `unknown-event` under version 8. Every version-9 record of `AuthorPanelRegistered` fails `unknown-event` under version 8, and every record carrying a version-4 ballot or a version-2 delivery record `event-data`. Version 9 freezes with its vectors.

A board that accepts ballot version 4 or a version-2 delivery record emits them into the sequence every election on it shares, and version 8 frames neither; an election registry with `registerDefinitionWithAuthorPanel` emits `AuthorPanelRegistered` into its own shared sequence, which version 8 doesn't name: each election pinning version 8 would fail 4.4 at the first such record or registration, whatever election it is for (T-53). So version 8 MUST be retired, as versions 1 to 7 must, before any such board or registry is deployed, and the election registry deployed with them MUST refuse a profile pinning any of versions 1 to 8, as [[verifier]] 2.3 must. Until then nothing reads version 9: the deployable registry still pins version 8 and refuses every definition pinning ballot version 4 (Election registry, Registration, rule 9), `packages/contracts` builds the board's version-4 and version-2 checks and the author registration with version 9's vectors checking the events they emit ([[P2-16]] part 5c), `packages/crypto` reads version 9 since part 5d-2, the verifier reads `AuthorPanelRegistered` in 3.1 since part 5d-3a and the rest of version 9 (2.3, 4.4, both ballot paths, 4.6 and 5.9's single version-2 records) since part 5d-3b, and in part 5e rule 9 pins version 9 for every definition version it serves, and serves definition version 6. [[verifier]] 2.3 passes version 9 wherever it passes version 8, and fails a profile that pins ballot version 4 beside version 8, which can't frame its ballots, since part 5e-5a, before rule 9 serves version 6; 5.9 reads its version-2 batches since part 5d-3c-1 and leaves a version-2 root undecided until part 5d-3c-2 reads them.

**Event tables (version 10).** Version 9's rows, every one unchanged, and the board's rolling ballot event ([[P2-17]] part 2b-2b; Board, Rolling ballots and Events):

| Role    | Event signature                                         | Indexed                        | Stateless checks on the data                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| ------- | ------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `board` | `RollingBallotSubmitted(bytes32,bytes32,bytes32,bytes)` | `definitionHash`, `ballotHash` | `root` is a canonical `field<bn254>` word other than 0, the roots the board's membership check takes (Membership proofs, `signal` and `root`); `record` is a ballot record of version 5 the board frames (What the board frames (version 10)), and the topics are its `definition_hash` and its `ballot_hash`, recomputed from its bytes, as for `BallotSubmitted`. Whether `root` is the root of the step the ballot names is [[verifier]] 5.1's (`root-step-mismatch`) |

**What the board frames (version 10).** Version 9's framing (What the board frames (version 9)), unchanged for every event version 9 names, so `BallotSubmitted` and `RelayedBallotSubmitted` still carry ballot versions 1 to 4 only, and for the rolling event a ballot record of version 5 only, framed as Rolling ballots frames it without the registries: record type `0x0002` and version 5; step 4's fixed fields as version 4's (`cell` any `u8`, `nullifier` below `r`); the `step` at 364, any `u32`; the committed vote at 368, framed as version 9 frames version 4's in its six fields, with nothing after it; and the membership proof's three points as under version 8. The ballot hash is recomputed from the record's bytes with the formula every other version uses, `H(DS("abolish/v1/ballot", record[3:35] ‖ record[0:3] ‖ record[35:68] ‖ record[364:]))`, which is Hashes (version 5)'s, since the step follows the nullifier there and starts the bytes from 364 on. So the version byte fixes which event can carry a ballot, and each carries one reading: a version-5 ballot in `BallotSubmitted`, a version-4 ballot in the rolling event, or, in the rolling event, a version-5 record relabelled version 4 (whose step would be read as the committed vote's first count), fails `event-data` (T-31); in `BallotSubmitted` such a relabelled record is another record, with another version byte and hash, never a second reading of the first.

So every record a board with the rolling call emits passes, since it emits a ballot only after its own check, which runs all of these and refuses the supplied roots the table refuses, and the check is never stricter than the board (T-53): a member's version-5 ballot whose element frames but isn't a point passes, as a version-4 one does, and so does one whose supplied root isn't the root of the step it names, which the verifier rejects later as an entry that counts nothing ([[ballot-validity]], Admission (version 5), `root-step-mismatch`).

Version 10 reads every record version 9 passes exactly as version 9 does, with the same fields, and fails every record version 9 fails with the same code, except a `RollingBallotSubmitted`, which version 9 doesn't name: every version-10 record of it, passing or not, fails `unknown-event` under version 9. Version 10 freezes with its vectors.

A board with the rolling call emits `RollingBallotSubmitted` into the sequence every election on it shares, and version 9 doesn't name it: each election pinning version 9 would fail 4.4 at the first rolling ballot anyone submits, whatever election it is for (T-53). So version 9 MUST be retired, as versions 1 to 8 must, before any such board is deployed, and the election registry deployed with it MUST refuse a profile pinning any of versions 1 to 9, as [[verifier]] 2.3 must. Until then no deployment emits version 10's new event: the deployable registry refuses every definition pinning ballot version 5 (Election registry, Registration, rule 9), `packages/crypto`'s codec and event check read it since [[P2-17]] part 3d, the verifier since part 4b-1 (which reports a profile that pins it unverifiable, `unsupported-version`, before then), and `packages/contracts` builds the rolling call in part 5, with version 10's vectors checking the event it emits.

**Event tables (version 11).** Version 10's rows, every one unchanged, and the board's relayed rolling ballot event ([[P2-17]] part 2b-2c-2; Board, Relayed rolling ballots and Events):

| Role    | Event signature                                                                            | Indexed                        | Stateless checks on the data                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| ------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------ | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `board` | `RelayedRollingBallotSubmitted(bytes32,bytes32,uint64,uint64,uint64,uint64,bytes32,bytes)` | `definitionHash`, `ballotHash` | The four call fields are canonical `uint64` words, as `RelayedBallotSubmitted`'s; `root` is a canonical `field<bn254>` word other than 0, as `RollingBallotSubmitted`'s; `record` is a ballot record of version 5 framed as the rolling event's is (What the board frames (version 10)), and the topics are its `definition_hash` and its `ballot_hash`, recomputed from its bytes. Whether the fields and root match a call the relay recorded is [[verifier]] 4.5's (`relay-unmatched`, L1 relay, Verification), and whether `root` is the root of the step the ballot names 5.1's (`root-step-mismatch`) |

**What the board frames (version 11).** Version 10's framing, unchanged for every event version 10 names, so `RelayedBallotSubmitted` still carries ballot versions 1 to 4 only and the rolling event version 5 only; the relayed rolling event carries a ballot of version 5 only, framed and hashed exactly as the rolling event's. Its data is `abi.encode(index, l1Block, stamp, gasLimit, root, record)`: six head words with the record's offset `0xc0`, so neither ballot event's data passes under the other's topic, nor `RelayedBallotSubmitted`'s (whose offset is `0xa0` and whose fifth word is that offset, never a root), and each record has one reading (T-31).

So every record a board with the relayed rolling call emits passes, since it emits only after its own check, which runs all of these, refuses the roots the table refuses and takes its four fields from a deposit's canonical calldata, and the check is never stricter than the board (T-53).

Version 11 reads every record version 10 passes exactly as version 10 does, with the same fields, and fails every record version 10 fails with the same code, except a `RelayedRollingBallotSubmitted`, which version 10 doesn't name: every version-11 record of it, passing or not, fails `unknown-event` under version 10. Version 11 freezes with its vectors.

A board with the relayed rolling call emits `RelayedRollingBallotSubmitted` into the sequence every election on it shares, and version 10 doesn't name it: each election pinning version 10 would fail 4.4 at the first rolling ballot anyone forces through L1, whatever election it is for (T-53). So version 10 MUST be retired, as versions 1 to 9 must, before any such board is deployed, and the election registry deployed with it MUST refuse a profile pinning any of versions 1 to 10, as [[verifier]] 2.3 must. Since a board that serves ballot version 5 needs both rolling calls (Board, Relayed rolling ballots), no registry ever pins version 10. Until then no deployment emits version 11's new event: the deployable registry refuses every definition pinning ballot version 5, `packages/crypto` reads it since [[P2-17]] part 3d, the verifier since part 4b-1 (which reports a profile that pins it unverifiable, `unsupported-version`, before then), and `packages/contracts` builds the relayed rolling call in part 5, with version 11's vectors checking the event it emits.

**Event tables (version 12).** Version 11's rows, every one unchanged but the trustee registry's `PanelPinned`, which version 12 replaces with the one that logs each panel's kind ([[P2-16]] part 5e-1; [[0027-trustee-panels-and-live-results]], Part 3; Trustee registry, Deployment and Events):

| Role               | Event signature                                | Indexed   | Stateless checks on the data                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| ------------------ | ---------------------------------------------- | --------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `trustee_registry` | `PanelPinned(bytes32,uint8,uint8,uint8,bytes)` | `panelId` | `threshold`, `quorum` and `kind` are canonical `uint8` words, `kind` 1 (`independent`) or 2 (`transitional`), the kinds the registry pins (Trustee registry, Deployment, check 7); `keys` as version 1's `PanelPinned` |

Its data is `abi.encode(threshold, quorum, kind, keys)`: four head words with the keys' offset `0x80`.
