Version 1's `PanelPinned(bytes32,uint8,uint8,bytes)` isn't in version 12's table, so a record of it fails `unknown-event`; its data under the new topic fails `event-data`, since its third word is the offset `0x60`, never a kind, and each record has one reading (T-31). Whether a panel's kind is the one the release pins for it is [[verifier]] 2.5's, as its quorum is 3.1's (Trustee registry, Events).

So every record a trustee registry that logs the kind emits passes, since it pins only kinds 1 and 2 and checks the keys as version 1's row does at deployment, and the check is never stricter than the registry (T-53).

Version 12 reads every record version 11 passes exactly as version 11 does, with the same fields, and fails every record version 11 fails with the same code, except the two `PanelPinned` events: every record of version 1's `PanelPinned`, passing or not, fails `unknown-event` under version 12, and every version-12 record of the kind-carrying one, passing or not, fails `unknown-event` under version 11. Version 12 freezes with its vectors.

A trustee registry that logs the kind emits its `PanelPinned` events at deployment, the first of the sequence every election shares (Trustee registry, Events), and versions 1 to 11 don't name that event: each election pinning one of them would fail 4.4 at the registry's first event, whatever election it is for (T-53). So versions 1 to 11 MUST be retired before such a registry is deployed, and the election registry deployed with it MUST refuse a profile pinning any of them, as [[verifier]] 2.3 must: it pins version 12 (Election registry, Registration, rule 9). `packages/crypto` reads version 12 since [[P2-16]] part 5e-3 and the verifier since part 5e-4; since part 5e-2 `packages/contracts`' trustee registry logs the kind and its election registry pins version 12, and version 12's vectors check the `PanelPinned` events the registry emits (`test/TrusteeRegistry.t.sol`).

**Event tables (version 13).** Version 12's rows, every one unchanged but the election registry's `DefinitionRegistered`, which reads the definition versions a registry that registers version 7 decodes ([[P2-17]] part 5a; Election registry, Registration, rule 2):

| Role                | Event signature                                                     | Indexed                        | Stateless checks on the data                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| ------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `election_registry` | `DefinitionRegistered(bytes32,bytes32,uint64,uint64,bytes32,bytes)` | `electionId`, `definitionHash` | `definition` decodes under the layout its version byte names, version 1 or 3 to 7, with its self-pin ([[election-definition]], Decoding), the registry's rule 2. Any other version fails, version 8 and every later one included; well-formedness and the hash are 2.1's |

Its signature, topics and data are version 2's, word for word: only the definition versions its `definition` may decode under change. A version-7 definition's registration fails `event-data` under versions 2 to 12, whose row decodes versions 1 and 3 to 6 only, and its event check can't change once a version freezes, so the definition version 7 adds ([[P2-17]], [[0028-rolling-electorates]]) needs a new event version for its registration, as the board's new calls did (Event tables (version 10) and (version 11)). A rolling definition and a fixed one are the same version and the same row: the electorate mode is the definition's, never the event's.

Version 13 reads every record version 12 passes exactly as version 12 does, with the same fields, and fails every record version 12 fails with the same code, except a `DefinitionRegistered` whose definition is a version-7 one that decodes, which version 12 fails (`event-data`) and version 13 passes. So every record a registry that decodes versions 1 and 3 to 7 emits passes, and the check is never stricter than the registry (T-53).

A registry that registers version 7 emits each registration into the sequence every election it creates shares (Election registry, Events), and versions 1 to 12 fail a version-7 one: the version-7 election itself would fail 4.4 at its own `DefinitionRegistered`, one of the events that name it and so are boarded ([[verifier]] 4.4), and every other election of that registry would find a record its version fails in the registry's archived sequence (4.4's replay, unverifiable, never a pass; T-53). So such a registry pins version 13 and refuses 1 to 12 (rule 9) for every definition version it serves, as [[verifier]] 2.3 must for a version-7 definition; an election of a version 1 or 3 to 6 that pins 12 is never registered there, and one on a registry that registers no version 7 keeps version 12, since its sequence holds no registration version 12 fails. Versions 1 to 12 are not retired: they stay valid wherever no version-7 definition is registered. No registry decodes version 7 yet: the deployed registry's rule 2 refuses it (`unknown-version`) and keeps pinning version 12 for the definitions it decodes, and a registry that registers version 7 is a new deployment ([[P2-17]] part 5). `packages/crypto`'s event check reads version 13 since [[P2-17]] part 5b, and the verifier since part 5c, from which [[verifier]] 2.3 fails a version-7 profile pinning contract-event version 8 to 12.

### Evidence

The only evidence archived for contract events is, per contract and per anchored period in which its count changed, and at close, the account proof and the storage proofs of `S` and `S + 1` at the first L2 block at or after the period's end, and no lower than the contract's previous record's block, for which an output root is posted on L1, with the output root's preimage, the block's header and the L1 dispute game that carries it ([[0025-contract-event-evidence]], Consequences and Amendment): one contract-event evidence record (`0x0025`) each, listed in a version-2 or version-3 archive manifest ([[archive]], Contract-event evidence). The events themselves are archived as contract-event runs (`0x0026`), each a run of one contract's consecutive records, listed by a version-3 manifest ([[archive]], Contract-event run). A verifier replays each contract's records against every proof (Event accumulator, Verification): rule 1 orders them, rule 2 places each on its side of the proof's block, and rule 3 shows that none is missing, added, altered or moved. Nothing per event is archived, so checking one event waits for the next proof (Known-weak below).

### Known-weak

- **Version 1 checks the trustee registry's first registration layout and names no board event.** Its `CeremonyRegistered` check is frozen with its vectors and predates the registered key set, so it fails every record the current registry emits with `event-data`, and a member's direct submission under it fails `unknown-event`. Version 2 reads both, and the election registry refuses a profile pinning version 1 (Election registry, Registration, rule 9), so no registered election is read under it; a registry that doesn't refuse it would let one direct submission fail its election through 4.4 (T-53). No registry or board has been deployed.
- **Version 2 checks a board record's form, not its meaning.** A record the board accepted for one election is in every election's shared sequence and passes the check whatever it says; whether it counts, and whether its signature, seat, cell or proof hold, is the verifier's ([[verifier]] 5; [[delivery]], Delivery check). So the archive keeps every chosen byte the board accepts (Board, Known-weak; T-17).
- **Version 2 names no event of the close rule.** A board with the relayed-ballot call or the statement call fails every election pinning version 2 at its first such event (Event tables (version 3)), so version 2 MUST be retired before one is deployed, and the registry refuses a profile pinning it (rule 9).
- **Version 3 names no batch event.** A board with the batch call fails every election pinning version 3 at its first batch (Event tables (version 4)), so version 3 MUST be retired before one is deployed, and the registry refuses a profile pinning it (rule 9).
- **Version 4 names no root event.** A board with the root call fails every election pinning version 4 at its first root (Event tables (version 5)), so version 4 MUST be retired before one is deployed, and the registry refuses a profile pinning it (rule 9). No trustee node sends a root yet ([[delivery]], Capacity), and the verifier doesn't read a root's leaves: a root naming an election holds its 5.9 unverifiable until [[P1-12f]] part 3 ([[verifier]], Counted set and tally, step 2).
- **Version 5 names no Tier 1 event.** A group registry with the Tier 1 call fails every election pinning version 5 at its first Tier 1 addition (Event tables (version 6)), so version 5 MUST be retired before one is deployed, and the registry refuses a profile pinning it (rule 9). No group registry may be deployed before P3-4 in any case.
- **Version 6 names no self-paid event.** A group registry with the self-paid calls fails every election pinning version 6 at the first self-paid group anyone creates (Event tables (version 7)), so version 6 MUST be retired before one is deployed, and the registry refuses a profile pinning it (rule 9, since [[P3-2]] part 5b-2).
- **Version 7 names no availability event.** A board with the availability call fails every election pinning versions 5 to 7 at the first attestation (Event tables (version 8)), so they MUST be retired before one is deployed, and the registry refuses a profile pinning them (rule 9, since [[P4-3]] part 2c-3).
- **Version 8 frames no version-4 ballot or version-2 delivery record, and names no `AuthorPanelRegistered`.** A board that accepts them, or a registry with the author panel's registration, fails every election pinning version 8 at the first such record (Event tables (version 9)), so version 8 MUST be retired before either is deployed. [[verifier]] 2.3 fails a profile pinning it beside ballot version 4 since [[P2-16]] part 5e-5a. Rule 9 pins version 12 since [[P2-16]] part 5e-2, and serves definition version 6 with ballot version 4 since part 5e-5b; `packages/contracts` checks the events it emits against version 9 since part 5c, `packages/crypto` reads it since part 5d-2 and the verifier since parts 5d-3a and 5d-3b, and 5.9 reads its version-2 batches since part 5d-3c-1, though it leaves its version-2 roots undecided until part 5d-3c-2.
- **Version 9 names no rolling ballot event.** A board with the rolling call fails every election pinning version 9 at its first rolling ballot (Event tables (version 10)), so version 9 MUST be retired before one is deployed. Rule 9 refuses every definition pinning ballot version 5 until [[P2-17]] part 5 builds the rolling calls and the registry that pins version 13; `packages/crypto` reads version 10 since [[P2-17]] part 3d, and the verifier since part 4b-1.
- **Version 10 names no relayed rolling ballot event.** A board with the relayed rolling call fails every election pinning version 10 at the first rolling ballot forced through L1 (Event tables (version 11)), so version 10 MUST be retired before one is deployed; a board that serves ballot version 5 has both rolling calls, so the registry deployed with it pins a version that names both, and refuses 1 to 12: version 13, which a registry that registers definition version 7, the only one pinning ballot version 5, pins (Event tables (version 13); [[P2-17]] part 5).
- **Versions 1 to 11 name no kind-carrying `PanelPinned`.** A trustee registry that logs the kind fails every election pinning one of them at its first event (Event tables (version 12)), so they MUST be retired before one is deployed, and the election registry deployed with it pins version 12 and refuses 1 to 11 ([[P2-16]] part 5e-2).
- **Versions 1 to 12 read no version-7 registration.** A registry that registers definition version 7 fails every election pinning one of them at 4.4 once it registers one: the version-7 election at its own `DefinitionRegistered`, every other in the registry's archived sequence (Event tables (version 13)). So such a registry pins version 13 and refuses 1 to 12 for every definition it serves ([[P2-17]] part 5); a registry that registers no version 7 keeps version 12. Until then no version-7 definition is registered anywhere: the deployed registry's rule 2 refuses it.
- **Version 9 checks an author registration's form, not its approvals.** Every registration the registry logs passes, and so would one short of its quorum or with seats out of order, which the registry never logs; the accumulator shows each record is the registry's, and [[verifier]] 3.1 is to judge the panel, stub, transcript, key set and approvals against the definition from [[P2-16]] part 5d, before any registry can emit one (T-37).
- **Version 6 checks a Tier 1 addition's form, not whether it counts.** Every addition the registry logs passes, junk naming made-up roots and copies of counted additions included, and each is decoded (six proofs' points, `B`'s subgroup check included) by every verifier that checks the registry's events (T-52). The counting rule decides what counts ([[vouching]]).
- **One event is checked only with its period.** Until the next storage proof covers it, a direct submitter's client sees its own event only through an L2 node it trusts (T-25), and must not stand down on it ([[delivery]], Client rules).
- **The output root is the root of trust for position and completeness** once L1 data has expired (T-71): a false, unchallenged TEE-only root can rewrite blocks and timestamps as it can the sequence. [[verifier]] 4.4 reports TEE-only roots.
- **Chosen bytes inside our events' own fields remain**, bounded by what each contract accepts: a creator's address and the free bits its creations buy, a registration's definition, a quorum's stub and transcript hashes and its key set's KEM-key hashes and signing keys, registrars' nonces and commitments (T-17's residuals list each).

## Testnet deployments

The **staging deployment** may precede nine conditions that this page, [[election-definition]], [[tally]], [[verifier]], [[0019-regional-cells]] and [[STATUS]] give as "before any registry is deployed" or "before any deployment" ([[0044-testnet-deployments]]). The staging deployment is one made for the `staging` environment only, on Base Sepolia (84532) and Sepolia (11155111), or on a local Anvil pair (L1 31337, L2 31338) for tests. The deploy workflow's input names it, its deployment files record it as staging, and only releases built for the staging environment bind it. Its polls are tests. Any deployment that serves public or production polls is not a staging deployment, whatever chain it is on, production on a testnet before mainnet included: it meets all nine and the owner's three residuals below. The nine are:

1. definition versions 1 and 3 refused (Election registry, Known-weak);
2. the registry's decoder budgeted to the record's limits under the L2's transaction gas cap (Election registry, Known-weak);
3. Tier 2's addition path ([[P3-4]]; Group registry, Known-weak);
4. the nullifier-key events in a contract-event version (Group registry, Known-weak);
5. a contract path for tally openings ([[tally]], Residuals, The operator can withhold every opening);
6. the binding of each deployment to the release creation code and its constructor arguments, specified ([[verifier]] 1.2), without which 1.2 stays `not-implemented` on the staging deployment as everywhere;
7. each group's region fixed at creation, logged in `GroupCreated` and bound by every signed addition and removal ([[0019-regional-cells]], Consequences), without which registration serves region 0 only, with one published group ([[registration]]);
8. the verifier reading obligation roots' leaves and their availability ([[P1-12f]] part 3), without which a root sent for an election holds it unverifiable (Board, the root call);
9. [[verifier]]'s `tally-late` built (5.6, 7.1).

Three residuals of [[delivery]] that must be closed or accepted by the owner before any election registry is deployed (Residuals: a newest ballot that never reached the board, approved stubs holding capacity, withheld board data and the complaint scope) are the owner's to accept for the staging deployment; it isn't made before the owner has accepted them or they are closed. Every other condition holds for the staging deployment as for any deployment. None of the nine is waived for any other deployment, and neither are the conditions this page gives as "before any registry is deployed beyond a testnet" (Group registry, Known-weak, Paid floods of self-paid additions), which bind every deployment but the staging one in the same way.

While any of the nine, or any of those, is open, the deploy script requires the explicit staging target and refuses any other target, and refuses every chain pair but Sepolia (11155111) with Base Sepolia (84532), or the local Anvil pair. A **release offered for real polls** is any verifier or ballot-client release not built for the staging environment. No such release binds the staging deployment, and none binds a deployment on chain 84532, 11155111, 31337 or 31338 while any of the nine is open. That is a rule of the release procedure ([[release]], Security notes), not something a contract can enforce, since anyone can deploy the same bytecode anywhere, and no release or verifier check enforces it yet. If 1.2's binding is later specified in a form an earlier deployment can't meet (a pinned factory, say), the staging deployment never passes 1.2, which costs only its tests. The staging deployment is never upgraded: closing a condition means a new deployment, and nothing carries the old one's elections forward.

## Election registry

Threats: T-34 (definition substitution), T-24 (equivocation), T-23 (board tampering), T-17 (chosen bytes in the archive), T-37 (a ceremony carried into another definition), T-08 and T-13 (an electorate that isn't public before voting), T-25 (blocking a legitimate election), T-71 (trusting who sent a transaction).

The election registry assigns each election its identifier and records the hash of its one definition ([[election-definition]]), write-once. It is immutable: no owner, proxy, pause or upgrade path, and it inherits the event accumulator above. Every rule below is fixed when it is deployed.

### Deployment

The registry is deployed with the deployment it belongs to, as immutable values: the `board`, `trustee_registry` and `group_registry` addresses on its own chain, the L1 chain ID, the `l1_anchor` and `l1_relay` addresses, and `epoch`, the anchoring clock's start of the anchor contract `l1_anchor` names (P4-1; [[election-definition]], The seal). Its own chain ID is `block.chainid` and its own address is `election_registry`. An L2 contract can't read the L1 anchor's clock, so `epoch` is an input: the deploy script MUST read it from the deployed `l1_anchor`, and the deployment record (`packages/contracts/deployments/<chain-id>.json`) MUST carry it, so a verifier can compare it with the clock its release pins. An `epoch` set too low only weakens the registry's early refusal, since [[verifier]] 2.6 still fails an election that opens before the real one; one set too high refuses legitimate elections until a corrected registry is deployed (liveness only). Deployment reverts unless these could appear in a well-formed definition (the `chain` rule: no zero address, the four L2 addresses including its own pairwise distinct, the two L1 addresses distinct, the L1 chain ID non-zero and different from its own), and, as for every contract, unless its chain ID fits a `u64`.

### Election identifier

```
election_id = H(DS("abolish/v1/election-id", u64(chain_id) ‖ bytes[20](registry) ‖ u64(counter)))
```

where `chain_id` is the registry's chain, `registry` its address and `counter` the number of elections it created before this one, from 0. The input after the tag is exactly 36 bytes. Binding the chain and the address makes the identifier unique across every registry, here or on another chain, so a redeployed registry can't reuse one and have its checkpoints attributed to another election ([[board]], `election_id`; T-23, T-24). It is a hash output, so the creator chooses none of its bytes, beyond the few bits that creating many elections and keeping one buys at the gas cost of each (T-17). Creation reverts rather than wrap the counter.

`createElection()` takes no arguments (calldata of exactly its 4-byte selector) and makes the caller the election's **creator**, the only account that can register its definition. Anyone can create an election. Doing so costs gas and blocks no other election's registration, because no one else can register under the identifier it gets; what it does cost others is below (Events, The shared event sequence). The identifier depends only on the counter, so if an L2 reorganisation reorders two creations, each creator ends up with the other's identifier. A creator MUST therefore wait until its `ElectionCreated` event is in finalized L2 history before putting the identifier in a stub and asking the panel for a ceremony; otherwise a reorg could hand the ceremony's identifier to someone else, who could then withhold its registration (T-25). They could only ever register the exact definition the panel approved.

### Registration

`registerDefinition(bytes definition)` registers the definition of an election. It checks, in this order, and reverts on the first failure:

1. **Canonical calldata.** The calldata is exactly the standard ABI encoding of one `bytes` argument: offset `0x20`, the length, the bytes, zero padding to a 32-byte boundary, and nothing after. Solidity's decoder accepts other offsets and trailing data, so without this a transaction could carry bytes no record defines ([[notation]], Result-critical fields; T-17).
2. **Strict decoding** of `definition` as record type `0x0001` under the layout its version byte names, version 1 or 3 to 6 (2 is never assigned, and any other is `unknown-version`), including the profile check of [[election-definition]] (Decoding), with the decode error codes of [[notation]]. The contract knows the record types specified when version 1 was frozen: `0x0007` and `0x0009` are `unexpected-record-type`, and every other type but `0x0001`, including any specified later, is `unknown-record-type`. A listed `p256-key` (version 4's receipt keys, version 6's author keys) is canonical only if its prefix is `0x02` or `0x03` and its `x`, below `p`, is the `x` of a point on P-256, which the contract checks with OpenZeppelin's audited code: `Math.modExp` gives the candidate square root `(x³ − 3x + b)^((p + 1) / 4)` (P-256's `p ≡ 3 mod 4`) and `P256.isValidPublicKey` checks the point; a key that fails is `non-canonical` at its first byte, as [[trustee-identity]] (Identity key) decodes it. A definition version a later release specifies is `unknown-version` to a deployed registry, which can't change: version 7 is registered only by a later registry, whose rule 2 decodes versions 1 and 3 to 7 and which logs each registration under contract-event version 13 ([[P2-17]] part 5; Contract-event record, Event tables (version 13)).
3. **Well-formedness**: every rule of [[election-definition]] (Well-formedness) for the definition's version, reporting the first that fails.
4. **This deployment**: `l2_chain_id` is the registry's chain, `l1_chain_id` the deployment's L1, `election_registry` the registry's own address, and `board`, `trustee_registry`, `group_registry`, `l1_anchor` and `l1_relay` the deployment's ([[election-definition]], What gets admitted).
5. **The election**: `election_id` was created by this registry, the caller is its creator, and it has no registered definition. One definition per election, write-once (T-34, T-24). Restricting registration to the creator stops anyone who learns a pending definition's fields (a trustee, whoever relays ceremony messages) from registering first and blocking it (T-25). This is the only check that uses the caller, and it guards only the creator's own identifier: the caller (possibly an aliased L1 sender, or a delegated account) is never trusted for anything else ([[notation]], Result-critical fields).
6. **Timing**: `opens_at ≥ epoch` (no ballot before the anchoring clock starts, [[verifier]] 2.6), `block.timestamp < opens_at` (registered before voting opens, [[verifier]] 2.1), and `MIN_INCLUSION_BOUND ≤ l1_inclusion_bound ≤ MAX_INCLUSION_BOUND` ([[parameters]]; [[close]]), else `InclusionBoundOutOfRange`. The bounds are constants of the registry's bytecode, never constructor inputs, so the code [[verifier]] 1.2 binds fixes them, and 2.6 checks δ against the bounds the release pins. The floor keeps an honest batcher's normal latency from failing an election at its close check (T-50, T-53), and the ceiling bounds the sequencer's silent rebuild window before close (T-35, T-45). Then `sequencing_window ≤ MAX_SEQUENCING_WINDOW`, else `SequencingWindowTooLarge`, and `max_sequencer_drift ≤ MAX_SEQUENCER_DRIFT`, else `SequencerDriftTooLarge` ([[parameters]]), also bytecode constants. [[verifier]] 2.6 requires the chain to have stayed within the definition's own values, so these ceilings keep any registered definition from assuming a forced-inclusion delay longer than the contract-event window was sized for (T-50, T-35) and so that 2.6 fails closed when L2 governance lets the sequencer lead L1 by more than Base's 30 minutes, the figure [[0004-l2-choice]]'s analysis and `ROOT_WINDOW`'s margin assume, rather than a creator's headroom absorbing the raise (T-35, T-71). The L1-origin checks (The L1 origin, above) use the release's pinned drift, never these values. They have no floor: a value below the chain's only fails its own election at 2.6. The same bytecode carries `CONTRACT_EVENT_WINDOW` and `BOARDING_WINDOW` ([[parameters]]), which no check here reads: the contract-event window `W_C` sized from `MAX_SEQUENCING_WINDOW` that fixes every registered election's counted extent ([[tally]], Tally window), so verifier 1.2's binding of this code fixes it for them all.
7. **The electorate is in the past**: every tier's `root_l2_block` (from version 3, each `tier_electorate`'s one block) is below `block.number`, the registering block ([[election-definition]], The electorate is in the past; T-08, T-13).
8. **The ceremony**: `ITrusteeRegistry(trustee_registry).ceremonyRecorded(stub_hash, ceremony_transcript_hash, panel_id, threshold, panel_size)` is true, where `stub_hash` is computed from `definition` and the other values are the definition's ([[election-definition]], Stub hash and the ceremony). The trustee registry returns true only for a pair that a quorum of distinct seats signed, of a panel it pins whose identifier, threshold and size are exactly those ([[trustee-identity]], Ceremony registration). Passing the panel fields means a quorum of one pinned panel can't approve a definition that names another panel or another threshold, even when the deployment pins more than one panel (a civic panel, [[0006-trustees]]; T-14, T-37); verifier 2.5 and the client's panel check still apply. The check compares hashes; it never relies on who sent either transaction (T-37, T-71). A transcript hash may repeat across definitions. Then, for a definition that carries a `poll_class` (version 6 and later), a class on the civic record (2, an organisational vote, or 3, a civic referendum) needs `ITrusteeRegistry(trustee_registry).kindOf(panel_id)` to be 1, `independent` (`CivicPanelNotIndependent`), so a poll under the transitional panel, or any panel the deployment logs as `transitional`, is never registered on the civic record ([[election-definition]], Poll class and results; Trustee registry, The kind; [[0027-trustee-panels-and-live-results]], Part 3; T-14). A class-1 poll runs under any pinned panel. Rule 9 serves version 6 since [[P2-16]] part 5e-5b, and the registry reads the kind from a trustee registry that logs it ([[P2-16]] part 5e-2); [[verifier]] 2.5's `panel-kind` still applies, against the kind the release pins.

9. **The profile** (`ProfileNotServed(record_type, version)`, naming the first pin refused, by record type): the profile pins exactly the versions this deployment serves for the definition's version, so no registered election leaves a voter's direct submission, a seat's close statement or a challenge without a path the board and the verifier read (T-25, T-50, T-42, T-53). The ballot (`0x0002`) is the version the board checks for the definition's version: 1 for definition version 1, 2 for versions 3 and 4, 3 for version 5, and 4 for version 6: the board checks ballot version 4 since [[P2-16]] part 5c-2 (Board, Ballots (version 4)) and every version-2 delivery record since parts 5c-3 and 5c-4 (Board, Delivery records (version 2)), `packages/crypto` and the verifier read contract-event version 9 and the author panel's registration since part 5d, and the registry serves version 6 since part 5e-5b. The spoiled-ballot opening (`0x0003`) is the one that opens that ballot's cores: 1 for ballot version 1, 2 for versions 2 and 3, 3 for version 4 ([[challenge]], Versions). The board entry (`0x0007`) is version 5 for a definition that is sealed and has no author panel (every definition before version 6), and version 7 for one that is sealed and has an author panel: of the versions that allow the close statement ([[close]], Version) as well as delivery records and destruction statements (5, 6 and 7), version 7 alone adds the author approvals of a definition with an author panel ([[ceremony]], Author panel), and [[verifier]] 2.3 fails an author panel beside any other version and any other definition beside it (T-17; served since [[P2-16]] part 5e-5d, once 2.3 passes a sealed author panel, part 5e-5c). For a live poll the registry serves none, so a live version-6 poll, with an author panel or without, is refused here whatever board-entry version it pins (`ProfileNotServed(0x0007, pinned)`), unless refused earlier at its tally-opening pin: version 6, which carries a live poll's running openings, needs a later registry, since 2.3 passes a live poll only for a release that pins enough of its default seats as ours, which the registry can't know, and fails a live poll with an author panel whatever it pins (the live restriction; T-16, T-45). The contract event (`0x0008`) is version 12, whose tables name every event the board emits, the close rule's, the batches', the obligation root's and the availability records', the group registry's Tier 1 addition and self-paid group and addition (Contract-event record, Event tables (version 8); [[P1-12f]] part 1b-2, [[P3-3]] part 3b-2a, [[P3-2]] part 5b-2, [[P4-3]] part 2c-3), and the trustee registry's kind-carrying `PanelPinned` in place of version 1's (Contract-event record, Event tables (version 12)), so no board or registry event fails a registered election at 4.4 (T-53). The registry is deployed with a trustee registry that logs the panels' kinds, so it refuses versions 1 to 11, which don't name that event ([[P2-16]] part 5e-2; until then it pinned version 8). The display text (`0x0009`) is version 2 for definition versions 5 and 6, which carry questions, and version 1 otherwise ([[display-text]]). A version a later release specifies is refused like any other, since a deployed registry can't learn it: a later board-entry, contract-event or ballot version that this deployment's board could serve needs a new registry. The tally opening (`0x0006`) is version 3, which sums an author panel's half beside the default half ([[tally]]), for a definition with an author panel, and any version but 2, which fixes a live poll's running opening, and 3 for one without: 2.3 fails an author panel beside any other version and any other definition beside version 3, and the registry serves no live poll, so 2.3 would fail version 2 beside every profile it serves (since [[P2-16]] part 5e-5b, and version 3 to an author panel since part 5e-5d; T-16, T-17). Checked before the board entry, so an author panel pinning another tally-opening version is refused here. The receipt and ceremony transcript pins, and a tally-opening version no release reads yet beside a definition without an author panel, are left to [[verifier]] 2.3: the board and the registry serve none of them, and 2.3 reports a version it doesn't read as unverifiable, never failed. The profile's own rules (`profile-types` and the rest) are rule 3's, and a definition version the registry doesn't decode is rule 2's.

It then stores `definition_hash` for `election_id`, records the board's parameters for `definition_hash` (Parameters, below) and emits `DefinitionRegistered`. A registered definition can't be changed or removed, and neither can its parameters.

The registry doesn't check what only the board can show: that each root is the root of its tier's additions at `root_l2_block`, each `group_size`, each group's tier, the panel against the pinned panels, or the version pins against a verifier release ([[verifier]] 2.3 to 2.5). Rule 9 refuses every profile [[verifier]] 2.3's `profile-combination` fails among the definition versions it decodes ([[delivery]], Versions; [[tally]], Destruction; [[ballot-validity]], Ballot record (versions 2 to 5); [[challenge]], Versions), and more: the combinations 2.3 reports unverifiable rather than failed, among them a contract-event or board-entry version a later release may read; 2.3 fails, as rule 9 refuses, a profile pinning a board-entry version without the close statement or contract-event version 1 to 7 ([[close]], Version). It serves ballot version 4 with definition version 6 since [[P2-16]] part 5e-5b, beside contract-event version 12, which frames it as version 9 does, and refuses ballot version 5 until the board checks it; definition version 7 is rule 2's `unknown-version`; a registry that registers ballot versions 4 and 5 comes with the board that checks ballot version 4 and the contract-event version that frames it, version 9 for ballot version 4 (Contract-event record, Event tables (version 9)) and version 13 for ballot version 5, which only a version-7 definition pins: version 11's tables frame both rolling calls' events and version 12's the kind-carrying `PanelPinned`, but only version 13's reads the version-7 definition's own registration (Contract-event record, Event tables (version 10) to (version 13); Board, Rolling ballots and Relayed rolling ballots; [[P2-17]] part 5), whose two rolling calls carry a rolling ballot's supplied root on the ordinary and the forced path (parts 2b-2b and 2b-2c-2), and which it serves only with ballot version 5's delivery records on their contract path (part 2b-2c-1, Board, Delivery records (ballot version 5); T-50, T-25). [[P2-16]] part 5a specifies the board's checks (Board, Ballots (version 4), Delivery records (version 2)) and the author key set's registration (Author panel registration); rule 9 serves definition version 6 since the rest of part 5 has built them, with a contract-event version that frames them (version 9 and later, [[P2-16]] part 5b-2), and a sealed author panel since part 5e-5d.

### Author panel registration

Threats: T-25, T-54 (an author seat with no contract path), T-37 (an approval replayed for another panel, stub or key set), T-14, T-17 and T-52 (chosen bytes and junk in the shared sequence). Decided by [[0035-author-key-set-on-l2]]; specified in [[P2-16]] part 5a, built with the rest of part 5. Rule 9 refused every author panel at its board-entry pin until [[P2-16]] part 5e-5d, which serves a sealed one board-entry version 7 and tally-opening version 3.

An author panel is never pinned in the trustee registry ([[ceremony]], Author panel), so the election registry records its key set itself, in the transaction that registers the definition, and the board reads it there (Board, Ballots (version 4), Delivery records (version 2)). The key set exists exactly when the election does: no ballot of an election with an author panel is ever checked against an empty author set (T-25, T-54).

`registerDefinitionWithAuthorPanel(bytes definition, bytes authorRegistration)` registers a definition whose `author_threshold` isn't 0. It runs rules 1 to 9 of Registration on `definition`, with rule 1's canonical calldata over its two `bytes` arguments (the head's two offsets exactly `0x40` and `0x60 + 32·⌈len(definition) / 32⌉`, then each argument's length, bytes and zero padding to a 32-byte boundary in order, and nothing after; any other layout reverts), then refuses a definition with no author panel (`AuthorPanelAbsent`), and then checks `authorRegistration` against the definition, in this order, reverting on the first failure (checks 6 and 7 run seat by seat, and 8 and 9 approval by approval, as the trustee registry's do). It runs checks 1 to 5 itself and has the trustee registry run 6 to 9, through `checkAuthorRegistration` (Trustee registry, Author registration check), passing the record, the points of `author_keys` in seat order and `k_A`, and letting the call's revert through unchanged, so each check fails with the error named below. The trustee registry is the contract every author seat's statement already names (check 9) and already carries OpenZeppelin's P-256 verification and the key-set code these checks share with its own registration; with them in its own code the election registry's runtime code would pass EIP-170's 24,576 bytes (about 26,200 in the build of [[P2-16]] part 5c-1). The trustee registry is a deployment address the definition names and rule 4 checks, immutable, and already trusted with the default panel's ceremony (rule 8), so this trusts nothing new. `registerDefinition` refuses a definition with an author panel after its rule 9 (`AuthorRegistrationMissing`), so no such definition is registered without its author key set.

`authorRegistration` has the trustee registry's registration layout (Trustee registry, Registration), so a reader decodes both with one decoder:

```
authorRegistration = panel_id ‖ stub_hash ‖ ceremony_transcript_hash ‖ u8(seats) ‖ seat × seats ‖ u8(count) ‖ approval × count
```

with `k_A` the definition's `author_threshold`, `n_A` the number of its `author_keys`, and `q_A = max(k_A, n_A − 1)` the author panel's approval quorum ([[ceremony]], Author panel, Approval), a constant of the bytecode, never an input.

1. **Length**: as the trustee registry's check 2: at least the 97-byte header, at least `98 + 97 · seats` bytes, and exactly `98 + 97 · seats + 65 · count`.
2. **The panel**: `panel_id` is `author_panel_id` ([[trustee-identity]], Author panel identifier), computed from `k_A` and the 33-byte encodings of `author_keys` as the definition carries them (`AuthorRegistrationMismatch(1)`).
3. **The stub**: `stub_hash` is `author_stub_hash`, computed from `definition` with both `ceremony_transcript_hash` and `author_transcript_hash` replaced by 32 zero bytes ([[election-definition]], Stub hash and the ceremony) (`AuthorRegistrationMismatch(2)`).
4. **The transcript**: `ceremony_transcript_hash` is the definition's `author_transcript_hash` (`AuthorRegistrationMismatch(3)`), which the `author-panel` rule makes non-zero.

   So the record's first 96 bytes carry nothing the definition doesn't fix (T-17).

5. **Quorum**: `count ≥ q_A`.
6. **The key set's size and seats**: `seats ≥ k_A` (`KeySetTooSmall`), and the seats' indices strictly increasing, the first at least 1 and the last at most `n_A` (`KeySetSeatsOutOfOrder`).
7. **The key set's signing keys**: each seat's `(x, y)` is a point of P-256 with `x, y < p`, checked with OpenZeppelin's `P256.isValidPublicKey` (`InvalidSigningKey`), and is neither an earlier seat's point nor the point of any of the definition's `author_keys` (`DuplicateSigningKey`). The registry holds no default panel's keys, so [[ceremony]]'s cross-panel `duplicate-key` against the default panel's identity keys is left to [[verifier]] 3.1, which compares the recorded set with its own transcript check.
8. **The approvals' seats**: strictly increasing, the first at least 1 and the last at most `n_A`, so each seat counts once (T-14, T-37).
9. **The signatures**: each approval's signature verifies, under the point of its seat's key in `author_keys` (rule 2 decoded it, and the election registry recovers its `y` as the square root rule 2's on-curve check computes, `(x³ − 3x + b)^((p + 1) / 4)`, or `p` minus it, whichever has the prefix's parity), on the ceremony registration statement ([[trustee-identity]], Ceremony registration) with `l2_chain_id = block.chainid`, `trustee_registry` the deployment's trustee registry (rule 4 makes it the definition's, which the author seats signed under), the record's `panel_id`, the approval's `index`, `stub_hash`, `ceremony_transcript_hash`, and `key_set_hash` computed from the record's seats ([[ceremony]], Key set hash). The trustee registry builds the 157-byte statement and its digest `H(DS("abolish/v1/sig/ceremony-registration-2", m))` itself, naming its own address, and verifies as for its own registrations: OpenZeppelin's `P256.verify` after checking `1 ≤ r < n` and `1 ≤ s ≤ (n − 1) / 2` itself ([[trustee-identity]], On chain). Every approval must verify, including any beyond the quorum.

The trustee registry's write-once check has no counterpart: rule 5 allows one definition per election, and the key set is recorded only with it.

The statement names the trustee registry, not this contract, because it is the statement every author seat already signs ([[ceremony]], Author panel, Approval). That binds no less: `panel_id` under the author tag differs from every default panel's, so no default seat's approval counts here and no author seat's counts in the trustee registry, and `stub_hash` binds the definition and its election (T-37). A record approved for another definition, panel, transcript or key set fails check 2, 3, 4 or 9.

It then records, for `definition_hash`, `key_set_hash`, the key set's seats and each one's signing key, beside the parameters (Parameters, below), and emits `AuthorPanelRegistered` after `DefinitionRegistered` (Events). The sender is never checked or recorded beyond rule 5's creator check (T-71).

**The author key set** is read with `authorKeySetOf(definition_hash)`, which returns `key_set_hash` and the seats as a bitmask (bit `i` set for seat `i`, so bit 0 never), and `authorSigningKeyOf(definition_hash, index)`, which returns author seat `index`'s signing key as `(x, y)`; both are zero for a seat outside the set and for a definition with no author panel or never registered. They are what `q_A` author seats signed, not what the transcript check returns: [[verifier]] 3.1 for the author panel compares the two and fails an election whose recorded set differs, as for the default panel (Trustee registry, Registration).

**What it adds to the archive.** One record per registered definition with an author panel, `98 + 97 · seats + 65 · count` bytes, at most about 2.7 KB at `MAX_TRUSTEES`. Its seats' `kem_key_hash`es, which no contract can check against a key, and signing keys, which need only be unused points, are about 64 chosen bytes per seat, and each approval's ECDSA nonce a few more, all signed by the author seats' identity keys and so attributable to them (T-17). Only a creator registering a real election adds one, so junk elections can't grow the sequence through it (T-52; [[0035-author-key-set-on-l2]], option A). Once recorded, the set also gives the author seats the board's contract path: like a default seat, each can archive any number of signed receipts and complaints, about 64 chosen bytes each, for gas alone, attributed to its key in `author_keys` (Board, Delivery records (version 2); Board, Known-weak). Author seats are the author's choice rather than trustees bound by [[0006-trustees]], so this extends that channel to parties the author picks; a member's postings are already a larger gas-only channel, so it adds no new kind of cost (T-17, T-52). That is within the author transcript's channel, which the same seats fill and [[0034-author-transcript-bytes]] accepted, and it carries only what [[ceremony]] (Key set hash) defines, never the transcript or its `ek`s, as that ADR requires.

**Gas.** One P-256 verification per approval on top of `registerDefinition`'s, about 250,000 gas each under OpenZeppelin's Solidity fallback and far less through the precompile (Trustee registry, Known-weak), one square root per author key, the call to the trustee registry with the record and the keys' points (a few thousand gas), and the key set's storage words. It runs once per election.

### Parameters

The board checks a direct submission against its election's definition ([[ballot-validity]], Residuals; [[delivery]], Direct submit), but takes nothing about the election from the submitter ([[0028-board-contract-checks]], 1C; T-25, T-71). So at registration the registry also records, keyed by `definition_hash`, what those checks read:

| Field                | Type                                      | Value                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| -------------------- | ----------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `ballot_version`     | `uint8`                                   | The version the definition's profile pins for the ballot (`0x0002`). Every pin is at least 1 (`profile-version`), so 0 means no definition with this hash is registered (`ballot-stray`).                                     |
| `threshold`          | `uint8`                                   | `k`, the default panel's threshold.                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `panel_size`         | `uint8`                                   | `n`, the default panel's size.                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `option_count`       | `uint16`                                  | `T`, every question's options together: the length of the committed vote ([[ballot-validity]], `validity-shape`).                                                                                                             |
| `cell_count`         | `uint16`                                  | How many cells the definition's partition has ([[election-definition]], Tally partition).                                                                                                                                     |
| `author_threshold`   | `uint8`                                   | `k_A`, the author panel's threshold; 0 with no author panel (version 6; The author panel, below).                                                                                                                             |
| `author_panel_size`  | `uint8`                                   | `n_A`, how many `author_keys` the definition lists; 0 with no author panel.                                                                                                                                                   |
| `opens_at`           | `uint64`                                  | The definition's `opens_at`.                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `closes_at`          | `uint64`                                  | The definition's `closes_at`.                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `l1_inclusion_bound` | `uint32`                                  | The definition's δ, within the bounds above. With `opens_at` and `closes_at` it is what the close rule's checks on the board read: the ordinary ballot's window and the statements' window ([[close]]; parts 4c-2b and 4c-3). |
| `questions`          | list of `(uint8, uint16, uint16, uint16)` | Each question's `method`, option count, `min_selections` and `max_selections`, in the order the ballot asks them.                                                                                                             |
| cells                | `(uint8, uint256)` each                   | Each cell's tier and root, the root its ballots prove membership against ([[membership]], Owned elsewhere), in partition order.                                                                                               |
| ceremony             | `(bytes32, bytes32)`                      | The `stub_hash` the registry checked the ceremony under (the default panel's, under version 6) and the definition's `panel_id`: where the trustee registry recorded the election's key set.                                   |

The table's order up to `questions` is the order of `parameters`' returned tuple. Four view calls read them, so the board reads only what one submission needs, never every cell of a large partition: `parameters(definition_hash)` returns every field but the cells and the ceremony, `cellTiers(definition_hash)` every cell's tier, in order, `cell(definition_hash, c)` cell `c`'s tier and root, reverting unless `c < cell_count`, and `ceremony(definition_hash)` the stub hash and panel, both zero for a hash never registered. Two more, `authorKeySetOf` and `authorSigningKeyOf`, read the author key set (Author panel registration). A ballot of version 1 names a tier, which the board finds among the cells' tiers (`ballot-tier`); one of version 2 or 3 names a cell by its position (`ballot-cell`).

Each definition version records them from its own layout:

- **Questions.** Versions 1, 3 and 4 have one question, `(1, option_count, min_selections, max_selections)`, method 1 being selection, which plurality is with the limits `(0, 1)`; versions 5 and 6 record their `questions` as listed. `option_count` is their total `T`, at most `MAX_OPTIONS` by `option-count`.
- **Cells.** Version 1's cells are its tiers, one per `electorate` entry in order, each with its tier and `root`. Versions 3 to 6 record every `tier_electorate`'s `cells` in order, tier by tier, each with its tier's label and its `root`: the partition order a ballot of version 2 or 3 names ([[election-definition]], Tally partition). Version 7 records them as version 6 does under `electorate_mode` 1, and under mode 2 one cell per `rolling_electorate` entry, in order, with its `tier` and the root 0: no root of a rolling poll is registered anywhere, so the rolling call takes the root from its submitter (Board, Rolling ballots) and every other ballot path refuses version 5 before it reads a root (rule 2 decodes no version 7 until [[P2-17]] part 5). `cells` bounds them at 256.
- **The ceremony.** The stub hash is the one registration already computes to ask the trustee registry whether the ceremony was recorded, so the board's key set is the one the registry admitted the definition on (Board, Delivery records; [[0028-board-contract-checks]], 2B). An author panel's ceremony isn't recorded in the trustee registry, which pins no author panel: its key set is recorded here, under the definition hash, by `registerDefinitionWithAuthorPanel` (Author panel registration).
- **The author panel.** `author_threshold` and `author_panel_size` are both 0 for a poll with no author panel and for every version before 6. The ballot that carries the author half is version 4 ([[ballot-validity]], Ballot record (version 4)), whose check reads them (Board, Ballots (version 4)), with the author key set `authorKeySetOf` returns.

The close rule's fields ([[close]]) are added before any registry is deployed (part 4c-2b), since a deployed one's can't change.

The parameters are a function of the registered definition alone, which `DefinitionRegistered` carries, so they need no event of their own. Counting never depends on them: [[verifier]] reads the definition, never the registry's storage, and a ballot the board admitted under a wrong parameter is still judged by the definition's own rules (5.1 to 5.4). Admission does: a wrong parameter would make the board refuse honest direct submissions, which the definition can't show, on the path a censored voter relies on (T-25). So the registry records them with the same code that decodes and checks the definition, and the tests compare each recorded field with the definition registered.

### Events

Each event goes through the event accumulator, with topic 0 the keccak-256 of its signature (the standard ABI):

| Event                                                                                                                                                                       | Topics                                       | Data                                                                                                                                                        |
| --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `ElectionCreated(bytes32 indexed electionId, address indexed creator, uint64 counter, uint64 l1OriginNumber, uint64 l1OriginTimestamp, bytes32 l1OriginHash)`               | `election_id`, the creator as a 32-byte word | `counter` and the block's L1 origin, ABI-encoded (128 bytes)                                                                                                |
| `DefinitionRegistered(bytes32 indexed electionId, bytes32 indexed definitionHash, uint64 l1OriginNumber, uint64 l1OriginTimestamp, bytes32 l1OriginHash, bytes definition)` | `election_id`, `definition_hash`             | The block's L1 origin, then the definition's full canonical encoding as `bytes`, ABI-encoded, so the board and any verifier rebuild it from the chain alone |
| `AuthorPanelRegistered(bytes32 indexed definitionHash, bytes32 indexed authorPanelId, bytes registration)`                                                                  | `definition_hash`, `author_panel_id`         | The author registration record whole (Author panel registration), ABI-encoded as `bytes`                                                                    |

`AuthorPanelRegistered` is emitted only by `registerDefinitionWithAuthorPanel`, right after the same call's `DefinitionRegistered`, so it never names a definition that isn't registered. Its topics repeat what the record and the definition fix; it carries no L1 origin, since nothing reads one from it. A verifier decodes the record as it decodes `CeremonyRegistered`'s, compares its `key_set_hash`, recomputed from the record's seats, with the hash of the key set its own transcript check of the author transcript returns, and counts its distinct author seats whose approvals verify under the definition's `author_keys`, with those of the author approvals records, against `q_A` ([[verifier]] 3.1 for the author panel; [[ceremony]], Author panel, The registration's approvals). No contract-event version up to 8 lists it: version 9, which frames ballot version 4, adds it, with its layout and its `authorPanelId` topic checked against the record (Contract-event record, Event tables (version 9)), and until a registry pins version 9 rule 9 refuses every definition that could emit it ([[P2-16]] part 5e).

**The L1 origin.** The first two events carry the L1 origin of the L2 block that emitted them: the number, timestamp and hash that the OP Stack's `L1Block` predeploy (`0x4200000000000000000000000000000000000015`) reports in the emitting call, as `uint64`, `uint64` and `bytes32`. The predeploy's address is a constant of the contract, never a deployment input, and on a chain without it both calls revert. The accumulator authenticates the three values with the rest of the event, so no L1-attributes transaction, header or receipt is archived for them ([[0025-contract-event-evidence]]). The creation's origin fixes the election's first segment `f` (Board anchor, Verification); the registration's is compared with a retired version's cutoff ([[versioning]], Old versions stay verifiable; [[verifier]] 2.3).

The registry doesn't check them, since it can't see L1, and a sequencer chooses a block's origin within `max_sequencer_drift`, so a verifier never takes them as read. For each of the two events it checks, failing 4.4 and naming the event otherwise ([[verifier]] 4.4):

1. `(l1OriginNumber, l1OriginTimestamp, l1OriginHash)` is one block of finalized L1: the block with that number has that hash and that timestamp, by the verifier's own trust in L1 headers ([[verifier]], Pinned trust roots);
2. `l1OriginTimestamp ≤ τ ≤ l1OriginTimestamp + D`, where `τ` is the event's L2 timestamp (Event accumulator, Step) and `D` the `max_sequencer_drift` the verifier's release pins for the deployment's L2 (1,800 seconds on Base, [[0004-l2-choice]]). Never the definition's own `max_sequencer_drift`: its creator chooses it, bounded above only by `MAX_SEQUENCER_DRIFT` ([[parameters]]) and free below ([[election-definition]]; [[verifier]] 2.6 only requires the chain to stay within it), so it can't bound its own registration's origin;
3. for `DefinitionRegistered`, the origin's number and timestamp are at or after those of the same election's `ElectionCreated`. Only the election's own two events are compared: other elections' events in the shared sequence are archived but never checked (The shared event sequence), so comparing with them would let one unchecked junk creation under a rewritten predeploy, naming a maximal origin, fail every later election through the registry.

Honest derivation passes with one exception: an L2 block's timestamp is at or after its origin's, and the OP Stack's derivation drops a batch whose non-empty block runs more than the drift ahead of its origin, which every block holding one of these events is, unless the event came from a deposit (a creation or registration forced through the L1 portal) in an epoch's first block with no batched transactions, which after an L1 block-production gap longer than the drift can be more than `D` past its origin and fail check 2 honestly (Known-weak). So neither the sequencer nor L2 governance rewriting the predeploy (T-71, T-69) can name an origin that isn't a real L1 block, or one more than `D` before the event's own block, or a registration origin earlier than its creation's. Within `D` an earlier origin moves `f` down by at most `⌈D / period⌉` periods (one on Base, with hourly periods), which the creator's per-slot check covers, since it starts from the same event's `f` (Board anchor, Verification, Pre-creation anchors), and moves a registration's origin back past a retired version's cutoff only if the cutoff is less than `D` before the registration (T-34, [[versioning]]). A client checks the creation's origin the same way before it takes `f` from it ([[board]], Segments).

**The shared event sequence.** One registry serves every election it creates, so its accumulator covers every creation and registration, including elections that never get a definition (anyone can create one, for gas). The contract-event record (`0x0008`, P1-18 part 4) and [[verifier]] 4.4 (P1-19) MUST therefore replay a shared contract's whole event sequence from the archive, authenticated by the accumulator's storage proof, and require board entries only for the events the election's checks use: those that name the election, each of its tiers' group creation and group additions and removals up to each `root_l2_block` (for a rolling tier, through close: Rolling tiers, below; for a Tier 1 group, exactly its counted additions, [[verifier]], Contract events, Tier 1 boarding), and its panel's key and ceremony registrations ([[verifier]] 4.4). Every other event of a shared contract need only be in the archive, which the deployment keeps for every event of each of its contracts, independent of any one election's board (P1-16, part 4). Otherwise every junk creation would have to be boarded on every live election's board, a cost that grows with elections times junk creations (T-52), or a verifier looking only at the election's own board would fail every election through that registry (T-53). Whatever a creation's transaction carries besides its 4-byte calldata (an outer call from the creator's own contract, an access list) never reaches the archive: each event is archived as its contract-event record alone, with no transaction or receipt (Contract-event record, Evidence).

**Rolling tiers.** Under definition version 7 with `electorate_mode` 2 ([[0028-rolling-electorates]]; [[P2-17]] part 2b-2a), a rolling tier's ballots prove against step roots taken during the poll, and its result reports counts and growth up to the close ([[membership]], Root clock and steps, Reported counts), so its required events don't stop at `root_l2_block`: for each group the tier lists, the board MUST carry the group's creation (`GroupCreated` or `SelfPaidGroupCreated`) and every one of its `MembersAdded`, `MembersRemoved` and `SelfPaidMemberAdded` in L2 blocks whose timestamp is at or before the definition's `closes_at` (for a Tier 1 group, exactly its counted `Tier1MemberAdded` in those blocks, [[verifier]], Contract events, Tier 1 boarding). L2 timestamps never decrease along the chain, so these blocks are a prefix of it, and the bound is the one every rolling reading uses: every accepted step's `τ_n` is at most `closes_at`, so its tree is over a subset of these events, and the close count, the eligible count and growth read events up to `closes_at` and none after. Every verifier computes these readings from the archive (2.4); the board carries their events for the reason it carries a fixed tier's up to `root_l2_block`, so that the board shows every event the result reads (4.4), and a shorter bound, such as the last accepted step's `τ_{n_c}`, would leave out the events of growth's last row. A group's events in later blocks need only be in the archive, as any other event of the registry, and boarding one fails nothing. The operator boards each one once its L2 block is final, as for a fixed tier ([[verifier]], Contract events, The operator's replay); a contract event is boarded late or not, after close included, as a close statement's is ([[close]], Close statement; [[tally]], Tally window), so the events of the poll's last blocks are boarded once those blocks are final, by the seal's contract-event window like every other contract event ([[election-definition]], The seal; `CONTRACT_EVENT_WINDOW`). Each is one board entry, so a rolling tier's board grows with its groups' additions during the poll as well as before it (Group registry, Self-paid additions, Junk; T-52, T-53).

A verifier takes the definition's registration from `DefinitionRegistered` on the registry its release pins, recomputes `definition_hash` from the bytes, and finds the election's creation, and no second registration, in the same contract's event sequence ([[verifier]] 2.1, 4.4). The creator's address is public; it is the account that runs the election, not a voter.

### Vectors

`vectors/election-registry.json` (format `abolish-election-registry-vectors/1`, see [[vectors/README|vectors]]) gives election identifiers and inputs with no `u64` encoding. TypeScript (`packages/crypto`) and Solidity (`packages/contracts`) tests both consume it; the Solidity tests also run every vector of `vectors/election-definition.json` through the contract's decoder, rules and hashes. Rule 9 has tests of its own, written from the spec: every served profile of definition versions 1 and 3 to 6 registers, whatever its receipt and ceremony-transcript pins and with tally-opening version 1, 4 or 5; tally-opening versions 2 and 3 are refused under every definition version; each served pin at any other version from 1 to 13 is refused with its record type and version, recording nothing, contract-event versions 1 to 11 among them; a live version-6 poll, one with an author panel, and one that is both, are refused at the board-entry pin whatever it pins; and a fuzz test registers a profile exactly when every served pin is served, refusing it at the first other pin in type order. The invariant suite registers definitions of versions 1 and 3 to 6 from any account and holds that a registration succeeds exactly for the creator, once. `test/ElectionRegistryAuthorPanel.t.sol` runs Author panel registration against a real trustee registry, through a test registry whose rule 9 also serves version 6's author panels (the deployed one refuses them there, which a test asserts): a definition registers with its key set and both events, recording each seat's signing key; every quorum `max(k_A, n_A − 1)` from 1-of-1 to 4-of-4; each check refused with its error, a header naming the default panel, another threshold, swapped seats, the default stub, another election's stub or the default transcript, approvals for another registry or chain, and any bit flip of the record, each recording nothing; `registerDefinition` refusing an author panel and the author call refusing a definition without one; and rule 8's kind, with a trustee registry pinning the default panel as each kind: classes 2 and 3 register under an `independent` panel and are refused `CivicPanelNotIndependent` under a `transitional` one, through both calls and after `CeremonyNotRecorded`, class 1 registers under either, and the deployed registry registers a sealed civic definition without an author panel under an `independent` panel and refuses it under a `transitional` one at rule 8. `test/ElectionRegistry.t.sol` registers every served profile of versions 1 and 3 to 6 (version 6 an everyday poll) under a panel whose kind is 0, which rule 8 never asks. `test/contract-events.test.ts` reads the version rule 9 pins from the registry's source and checks its tables name every event the election, trustee and group registries and the board declare, as their compiled ABIs give them (the group registry's two nullifier-key events, which no table names yet, aside). `test/ElectionDefinition.t.sol` checks `author_stub_hash` and `author_panel_id` against every vector of `vectors/election-definition-v6.json` and its `authorPanels`, and the key decompression against any key. `test/invariant/ElectionRegistryAuthorPanel.invariant.t.sol` registers definitions with and without author panels through both calls, with honest, short and corrupted records, and holds that `registerDefinition` never registers an author panel, an author key set is recorded exactly for the definitions registered with one and is the record's, each election keeps its first definition, and the accumulator counts two events per author registration.

### Known-weak

- **The sequencing ceilings are Base's values today** (Registration, rule 6). If Base raises its sequencing window or drift, which it can without delay ([[0004-l2-choice]]), every poll open while the raise holds fails [[verifier]] 2.6, unattributed, until a registry with new ceilings is deployed (T-50, T-53).
- **Creation is permissionless**, so anyone can make the counter grow. Each creation costs its sender gas and reserves nothing anyone else needs.
- **The L1 origin is checked only by verifiers and clients** (The L1 origin, above): the registry records what the predeploy reports, and within the drift the sequencer, or L2 governance rewriting the predeploy, chooses it (T-71, T-69).
- **The pinned drift must keep up with the chain.** If the L2 raises its `max_sequencer_drift` above the one a verifier release pins (Base can, without delay, [[0004-l2-choice]]), honest events whose origin lags by more than the pinned value fail 4.4, attributed to no one, until a release pins the new value; a pinned value raised past a period lengthens the creator's per-slot check to match. Liveness only.
- **A forced event can fail check 2 honestly.** A creation or registration sent as a deposit lands in its epoch's first block, which carries no batched transaction and so isn't bound by the drift: after an L1 block-production gap longer than `D` it can be more than `D` past its origin. Liveness only, and only for forced calls, which an honest creator makes only when the sequencer censors it (T-25); it creates again or registers later.
- **The registry depends on the predeploy.** L2 governance that makes `L1Block` revert stops every creation and registration, a denial of service it can already cause in other ways (T-69, T-25).
- **Versions 1 and 3 are still admitted.** [[election-definition]] requires both to be retired before any registry is deployed, so that every election a deployed registry admits binds receipt keys; the registry decodes them until then, and refusing them MUST come before any deployment but the staging one (Testnet deployments).
- **Rule 9 is this deployment's.** It serves exactly the versions this board reads and this verifier generation checks, as bytecode: a later board-entry version (version 6, which carries live results' running openings, [[tally]], Running opening record), or ballot version 5 with its definition, each needs a new registry (and a board for ballots), never a change to this one. Definition version 6's author-panel parameters (Parameters) and its author key set (Author panel registration) are recorded since [[P2-16]] part 5e-5d serves a sealed author panel; a registry deployed before it refuses every author panel, and so is one no release that passes author panels pins ([[verifier]] 2.3; T-36).
- **Large definitions may not fit one transaction.** Decoding and checking a version-6 definition with one tier at its list limits (1,024 groups and regions, about 29 KB) costs about 7.7 million gas, mostly memory; with all three tiers at their limits and 256 cells (about 66 KB, the largest that passes `cells`) about 29 million, and registering it, with its calldata, event, hashes and up to 256 stored roots, about 37 million or more. A typical version-6 definition costs about 96,000. If the L2 caps a transaction's gas (EIP-7825 sets `2^24`, about 16.8 million), a well-formed definition much above half a full tier per tier can never be registered, and a deployed registry can't change (T-54, liveness). Before any registry but the staging deployment's is deployed (Testnet deployments), the decoder MUST be made cheap enough for the record's limits under the target L2's cap, or the limits lowered in a later definition version; a test at the three-tier limits then fixes the budget. Only the creator pays, once per election.
- **Gas.** Registering a two-tier definition (about 500 bytes) costs about 350,000 gas in the tests: about 125,000 for decoding, the ceremony check, three SHA-256 calls (stub, definition, accumulator) and the three reads of the L1 origin, and about 225,000 for the ten storage words of its parameters and ceremony (Parameters), one more for each further cell's root and one for each further 32 cells' tiers; creating an election costs about 108,000. Each runs once per election, so neither is a hot path.

## Trustee registry

Threats: T-71 (trusting who sent a transaction), T-37 (a ceremony carried into another definition, a stolen identity key), T-14 (fewer than `k` parties opening ballots), T-31 (malleable signatures), T-17 (chosen bytes in the archive), T-25 (blocking a legitimate election).

The trustee registry pins the deployment's trustee panels and records, for each definition stub, the one ceremony transcript a quorum of a panel's seats approved. The election registry asks it `ceremonyRecorded` before admitting a definition (Election registry, rule 8). It is immutable: no owner, proxy, pause or upgrade path, no way to add, change or remove a panel, and it inherits the event accumulator above.

### Deployment

The registry is deployed with a non-empty list of panels. For each it takes the threshold `k`, a **quorum** `q`, its **kind**, the seats' keys as their `p256-key` encodings in seat order ([[trustee-identity]], Identity key), and each key's point `(x, y)`. It refuses the deployment, naming the panel's position and the first failing check, in this order (checks 3 and 4 run seat by seat, so a seat's key is checked, then compared with the earlier seats', before the next seat's):

1. `n ≤ MAX_TRUSTEES`, where `n` is the number of points (`size`);
2. `2 ≤ k ≤ n` (`threshold`);
3. the encodings are exactly `33 · n` bytes, and each seat's encoding is the encoding of its point: first byte `0x02 + (y mod 2)`, the same `x`, and `(x, y)` on the curve with `x, y < p`, checked with OpenZeppelin's `P256.isValidPublicKey` (`key`). An encoding passes this for some point exactly when `p256-key` accepts it, so the deployment pins exactly the keys a verifier decodes, the two points with `x = 0` included;
4. no point repeats an earlier seat's (`duplicate-key`);
5. `k ≤ q ≤ n` (`quorum`);
6. its `panel_id` ([[trustee-identity]], Panel identifier), computed from `k`, `n` and the encodings, isn't an earlier panel's (`duplicate-panel`). Another quorum or kind doesn't make another panel;
7. its kind is 1 (`independent`) or 2 (`transitional`), the deployment record's `kind` ([[deployment]], Record) (`kind`).

Checks 1 to 4 are [[trustee-identity]]'s panel rules, with its codes. Each pinned panel is logged as `PanelPinned`, so the board and any verifier read the deployment's panels, quorums and kinds from the chain alone. As for every contract, deployment reverts unless the chain ID fits a `u64`, which the registration statement's `l2_chain_id` needs.

**The quorum.** `q` is how many distinct seats must sign a registration. It is at least `k`, because a transcript fewer than `k` seats approved could register keys no `k` honest seats checked, and at most `n`. A deployment sets it per panel, and its deployment record (`packages/contracts/deployments/<chain-id>.json`) MUST carry it. Every deployment MUST set `q = max(k, n − 1)` for every panel it pins ([[0006-trustees]] rule 7, adopted by the owner on 2026-10-12: an election opens only when at least `n − 1` seats, and never fewer than `k`, take part). The contract itself still accepts any `k ≤ q ≤ n`, so the rule binds the deployment and the release: a release pins `q = max(k, n − 1)` for each panel it pins, [[verifier]] 3.1 requires that many approvals in `CeremonyRegistered` whatever the contract's `q`, and a release MUST NOT pin a deployment whose `PanelPinned` events show another `q` for any panel. A deployment with a lower `q` could only register a transcript the verifier then fails (an election discredited and stalled, never one with weaker secrecy); checking `q` in the constructor would remove even that. A panel pinned with a lower `q` would let fewer seats register a transcript, never fewer than `k`. The participation rule is also enforced where it matters for the result, whatever `q` is: an honest seat approves only a transcript with at least `max(k, n − 1)` qualified seats ([[ceremony]], Approval and re-runs), and [[verifier]] 3.2 fails any other (`ceremony-participation`); that is a property of the transcript, not of who approved it.

**The kind.** A panel's kind says whether it may hold a poll on the civic record: only an `independent` one may ([[0027-trustee-panels-and-live-results]], Part 3; [[election-definition]], Poll class and results). `kindOf(panel_id)` returns a pinned panel's kind, and 0 for a panel the registry doesn't pin, and the election registry refuses a civic-record definition whose default panel's kind isn't 1 (Election registry, Registration, rule 8), so a poll under the transitional panel can't be registered on the civic record, not only fail its verification (T-14). Like the quorum, the kind is a constructor input held in storage, which [[verifier]] 1.2's bytecode check doesn't pin, so it binds the deployment and the release: a deployment MUST log each panel with the kind its deployment record gives it ([[deployment]], `panel-kind`: an `independent` panel has at most one seat of ours), a release MUST NOT pin a deployment whose `PanelPinned` events show another kind for any panel it pins, and [[verifier]] 2.5 judges `panel-kind` by the kind its release pins, never the logged one (Known-weak).

### Registration

`registerCeremony(bytes registration)` takes one record:

```
registration = panel_id ‖ stub_hash ‖ ceremony_transcript_hash ‖ u8(seats) ‖ seat × seats ‖ u8(count) ‖ approval × count
seat         = u8(index) ‖ kem_key_hash ‖ x ‖ y
approval     = u8(index) ‖ p256-sig
```

`panel_id`, `stub_hash`, `ceremony_transcript_hash` and `kem_key_hash` are `bytes[32]`, and `p256-sig` is [[trustee-identity]]'s 64-byte `r ‖ s`. The `seat`s are the registered key set ([[ceremony]], Key set hash), each with its signing key as the point `(x, y)`, two 32-byte big-endian coordinates, rather than its 33-byte encoding: the contract checks a signature against the point and can't decompress an encoding without implementing a square root, so it takes the point and computes the encoding, `u8(0x02 + (y mod 2)) ‖ x`, which is the only one that point has. The record is exactly `98 + 97 · seats + 65 · count` bytes. Anyone may submit it. The registry checks, in this order, and reverts on the first failure (checks 7 and 8 run seat by seat, and 9 and 10 approval by approval: a seat's or approval's checks, in order, before the next one's):

1. **Canonical calldata**, as for the election registry's `registerDefinition` (T-17).
2. **Length**: at least the 97-byte header, at least `98 + 97 · seats` bytes, and exactly `98 + 97 · seats + 65 · count`. Nothing can be appended, so the record carries no bytes the layout doesn't define (T-17).
3. **The panel** is pinned.
4. **The transcript hash** isn't zero, the value a stub carries in its place ([[election-definition]], Stub hash and the ceremony).
5. **Quorum**: `count ≥ q`.
6. **Write-once**: nothing is recorded yet for this `stub_hash` and panel.
7. **The key set's size and seats**: `seats ≥ k`, so the panel can open a ballot ([[ceremony]], Transcript check, 5) (`KeySetTooSmall`), and the seats' indices are strictly increasing, the first at least 1 and the last at most `n` (`KeySetSeatsOutOfOrder`).
8. **The key set's signing keys**: each seat's `(x, y)` is a point of P-256 with `x, y < p`, checked with OpenZeppelin's `P256.isValidPublicKey` (`InvalidSigningKey`), and is neither an earlier seat's point nor any pinned identity key of the panel (`DuplicateSigningKey`).
