- **A rewritten L2 history** (T-71): L2 governance or a false output root can show a chain on which a different initial key, or a different successor, was recorded, from any quorum's still-valid approvals placed before their deadline. The verifier's replay catches a history the registry couldn't have logged, and only the accumulator's storage proofs catch the rest (4.4, the T-71 residual), as for the group records.
- **The margin assumes Base's sequencing window.** `FORCE_LEAD` and `RECORD_MARGIN` are counted from `F`, which assumes the chain's sequencing window stays within `MAX_SEQUENCING_WINDOW` and L1 includes the deposit within `F`; Base can raise its window without delay ([[0004-l2-choice]]), and a sequencer then censoring the record past a deadline costs a run (T-54).
- **A key can be withheld or raced.** Whoever holds a quorum's approvals can withhold the record past the deadline, costing the run (T-54), and two runs that both reach a quorum race to be recorded; the loser's approvals are dead. Both cost liveness only.
- **The nullifier-key events aren't in a record version yet** (T-54): the registry doesn't stop a quorum of its initial panel recording a key before the contract-event version that lists `NullifierKeyRecorded` and `NullifierKeyReshared` exists, and every reader of the registry's sequence under an earlier version would then refuse that log (`unknown-event`), stopping sealing, the tier replays and the Tier 1 batches for every election naming its groups. Only procedure prevents it until that version, which [[P3-4]] adds with the Tier 2 additions, before any registry is deployed beyond a testnet; a quorum that would do it can already withhold its decryption shares, so it costs liveness only.
- **Tier 2 has no addition path yet** (P3-4), and no registry but the staging deployment's may be deployed until it does (Testnet deployments). Until then no Tier 2 group exists, so every Tier 1 addition reverts at the latest at its first vouch's `tier2_group_id` (Tier 1 additions, rule 5).
- **Only our batches are sponsored.** The registration paymaster (below) pays for the signing service's `addMembers` calls only; anyone else who submits an addition pays its gas, and the API pays for the Tier 1 additions it relays (Tier 1 additions, Who pays).
- **Junk Tier 1 additions are still loggable** (T-52): an addition whose vouches name made-up roots passes every check the registry makes, for any group and commitment, and each is one more event in the shared sequence. Its sender pays about 1.5 million gas for it, and a verifier refuses it at `root-mismatch`, after decoding it, without a pairing (Tier 1 additions, What the checks buy).
- **Junk Tier 1 additions carry chosen bytes** (T-17): an addition naming a made-up root can carry any `C_V` below `r` that isn't forbidden, about 31 bytes of the sender's choosing in the archive for about 1.5 million gas; its other fields are hashes or proof points the sender can only grind.
- **Copies of counted Tier 1 additions are loggable too** (T-52): the registry keeps no slot or commitment state, so anyone can log a verbatim or re-randomised copy of any counted addition, without bound, at about 1.5 million gas each. A verifier refuses each at `slot-spent` or `member`; only one that checks proofs last spends no pairing on them ([[vouching]], Batched verification, Proofs last).
- **A Tier 1 member can't be removed** (Tier 1 additions, No Tier 1 removals): a lost or stolen Tier 1 secret stays a member for the registry's life, and its holder can vote with it (T-69). Re-vouching gives the person a fresh identity, not the old one back.
- **Gas.** In the tests, adding one member with two of three approvals costs about 67,000 gas beyond the transaction's base cost and calldata, each further commitment in the same batch about 1,500, and a batch of 256 about 430,000: two `ecrecover` calls, the keccak hashes of the digest, the storage write and the accumulator step. Removing one member costs about 85,000, mostly its first write to the removed-index bitmap.

## Registration paymaster

Threats: T-13 (linking an identity to who sponsored it), T-52 (spending our gas), T-62 (contract admin abuse), T-69 (a seized service), T-71 (trusting who sent a transaction), T-17 (chosen bytes).

[[0004-l2-choice]] has registration paid by our own [ERC-4337](https://eips.ethereum.org/EIPS/eip-4337) paymaster, never a hosted one, and [[0024-tier0-registration]] has it sponsor only the group registry's `addMembers` from the signing service ([[registration]], The batch). This contract is that paymaster, for EntryPoint v0.8 (whose address the deployment record names). It pays for the service's batches and nothing else: no voter is ever a sponsored sender, so the paymaster, its bundler and the chain see the service's account next to each batch, whose commitments the registry's event publishes anyway, and never a voter's account or address (T-13). It is no part of what a verifier checks: it holds nothing a verifier reads and emits no log (the EntryPoint logs each operation), so it doesn't inherit the event accumulator.

### Deployment

The paymaster pins, as immutables:

- `entryPoint`, which holds its deposit and is the only caller of its validation;
- `registry`, the group registry whose `addMembers` it sponsors;
- `service`, the signing service's account (Registration account, below), deployed by an unsponsored transaction before use;
- `admin`, the `TimelockController` behind our Safe (contracts skill, Rules), never a single key;
- `maxCost`, the most one operation may cost the deposit, in wei.

Deployment reverts if the EntryPoint, the registry or the admin has no code, or only an EIP-7702 delegation (`0xef0100 ‖ address`, a single key's account; code doesn't prove a timelock, so the deployment record names it), if `service` is zero, or if `maxCost` is zero (T-62). Nothing can be changed later: a new service account or cap is a new paymaster, funded by withdrawing from the old one. Its sponsorship rules being fixed is what lets the admin be trusted with funds alone.

### Sponsorship

`validatePaymasterUserOp(userOp, userOpHash, maxCost)` reverts unless its caller is the EntryPoint (`NotEntryPoint`), then checks, in this order, reverting with `NotSponsored(rule)` on the first failure:

1. **`Sender`**: `userOp.sender` is `service`. No one else is sponsored, even for a well-signed record, since every operation costs the deposit whether or not its call succeeds (T-52, T-71).
2. **`InitCode`**: `initCode` is empty, so the deposit never pays for deploying an account.
3. **`PaymasterData`**: `paymasterAndData` is exactly 52 bytes, the paymaster's address and its two gas limits (EntryPoint v0.8's `PAYMASTER_DATA_OFFSET`), with no data after them.
4. **`CallData`**: `callData` is exactly the standard ABI encoding of `execute(address,uint256,bytes)` (selector `0xb61d27f6`, the call of ERC-4337's reference `BaseAccount`) with the target word `registry` (its upper 96 bits zero), the value word 0, the offset word `0x60`, a length that fits, nothing after the data's zero padding, and data that is itself exactly the standard ABI encoding of `addMembers(bytes)`: its selector, the offset word `0x20`, the record's length word and the record padded to a word, so that the data's length is `68` plus the record's length rounded up to 32. With the offsets and lengths fixed, the call checked is the one the account makes, and the data carries nothing after the record, which the registry would refuse (canonical calldata) at the deposit's cost. The record after the selector is the registry's to judge (Group registry, Additions): the paymaster can't run the registry's checks, whose state ERC-7562 forbids it to read (T-17).
5. **`Cost`**: `maxCost` is at most the paymaster's `maxCost`.

It then returns an empty context and a validation data of 0 (no signature failure, no time range). With no context, the EntryPoint never calls `postOp`, which always reverts. Every check reads the operation and immutables only, never storage, so the paymaster meets ERC-7562's rules for an unstaked paymaster; a stake only lifts bundlers' throughput limits.

### Funds

Anyone may add to the deposit with `deposit()`, which forwards to the EntryPoint's `depositTo`. Only the admin may call `withdrawTo(to, amount)`, `addStake(unstakeDelaySec)`, `unlockStake()` and `withdrawStake(to)`, which forward to the EntryPoint (T-62). The paymaster holds no ether itself and has no `receive`.

### Known-weak

- **A seized service drains the deposit** (T-69, T-52): whoever holds the service's account key can act as their own bundler, naming themselves the `handleOps` beneficiary, and set gas limits and fees so each operation's charge approaches `maxCost`; the EntryPoint pays that charge (and its penalty on unused gas) from the deposit to them, whether the record is valid or refused. So the deposit is at the service key's disposal, `maxCost` per operation. Keep it to a few days' batches and top it up; the batch job's spending is visible on chain. A per-period limit would need the paymaster to write storage, which ERC-7562 allows only to a staked paymaster.
- **The account's `execute` is assumed.** The paymaster reads `execute`'s calldata as ERC-4337's reference account decodes it; a service account whose `execute` means something else would be sponsored for whatever it does. The deployment record names the account and its code, which MUST be the registration account below: the reference `SimpleAccount` is UUPS-upgradeable, and an EIP-7702 account can be re-delegated, so with either the owner key could change what `execute` does later. Either way the loss is bounded as above.
- **Front-running costs the deposit.** `addMembers` is permissionless, so anyone who sees a sponsored operation before it lands can submit its record directly; the sponsored operation then reverts at the registry (`position`) and is still charged. The griefer pays about as much gas and the members land anyway, so nothing is amplified; submitting through our own or a private bundler avoids it.
- **The bundler sees the service's operations**, with their network metadata. They carry only what the batch's event shows, so a bundler learns nothing about registrants; running our own bundler, or submitting through a public one, is a deployment choice ([[0004-l2-choice]], Paymaster).

## Registration account

Threats: T-62 (contract admin abuse), T-69 (a seized service), T-52 (spending our gas).

The signing service's [ERC-4337](https://eips.ethereum.org/EIPS/eip-4337) account, the registration paymaster's `service`, from which it submits each Tier 0 batch ([[registration]], The batch). It is eth-infinitism's reference `BaseAccount` for EntryPoint v0.8 (account-abstraction `v0.8.0`), unmodified, with one owner and no upgrade path, so that `execute` is exactly the reference account's, the call the paymaster's rule `CallData` reads, and stays so. Like the paymaster, it is no part of what a verifier checks and emits no log.

### Deployment

The account pins, as immutables, `entryPoint` (EntryPoint v0.8, the deployment record's) and `owner`, the address of the signing service's key. Deployment reverts if the EntryPoint has no code or only an EIP-7702 delegation, or if `owner` is zero. Nothing can be changed later: it has no proxy, no upgrade entry, no `delegatecall` and no way to change its owner, so a new key is a new account and a new paymaster (T-62).

### Operations

- `validateUserOp(userOp, userOpHash, missingAccountFunds)` reverts unless its caller is the EntryPoint. It returns validation data 0 if `userOp.signature` is 65 bytes `r ‖ s ‖ v` with `s ≤ (n − 1) / 2` and `v` ∈ {27, 28} and recovers, over `userOpHash` as given (EntryPoint v0.8's EIP-712 digest of the operation, which binds the chain ID, the EntryPoint and the nonce), to `owner`, and 1 (`SIG_VALIDATION_FAILED`) otherwise, never reverting on a bad signature. It sets no time range and pays `missingAccountFunds` from its balance. The account has no `receive`, so it holds no ether unless someone sends it some (an EntryPoint deposit to it, or a forced transfer); such funds are the owner key's to spend and grant no authority anywhere, and without them every operation it sends must name a paymaster.
- `execute(target, value, data)` and `executeBatch(calls)` revert unless their caller is the EntryPoint, and make the call (or calls) as given, bubbling a single call's revert. The paymaster sponsors only `execute(registry, 0, addMembers(record))`; anything else the owner signs pays for itself, from whatever the account has been sent.

### Known-weak

- **The owner key is online** in the signing service, beside the registrars' keys (T-69). Whoever holds it can spend the paymaster's deposit as above; it can't add a member without a threshold of registrars' approvals, which the registry checks.
- **Only the tests run the vendored EntryPoint.** They compile its v0.8 source with this package's settings, so its bytecode differs from the canonical deployment's; a deployment uses the canonical EntryPoint v0.8 at the address the deployment record names.

## Board (direct submit)

Threats: T-25 (a ballot or delivery record kept off the board), T-05 (an unsound proof check), T-31 (two readings of one proof), T-17 (chosen bytes in the archive), T-52 (input that costs others work). Decided by [[0028-board-contract-checks]].

The `board` contract on L2 is the direct-submit path: anyone submits a ballot there, bypassing our servers ([[0004-l2-choice]]), and every share receipt, complaint and posting has its contract path there ([[delivery]], Direct submit). It is immutable, inherits the event accumulator above, and emits its events as contract-event records ([[0025-contract-event-evidence]]). It is specified and built in parts of P1-18 part 4b-3 ([[0028-board-contract-checks]], Consequences): the membership check and the ballot check below, the parameters they read from the election registry (Election registry, Parameters), the registered key set the trustee registry records (Trustee registry, Registration), which gives the ballot check its qualified seats and the delivery paths their signing keys, the contract's deployment, delivery checks, calls and events (4b-3d-1), and the contract-event record's version 2, which archives its events (4b-3d-2; Contract-event record, Event tables (version 2)). The close rule (part 4c) brings the relayed-ballot call, the close statements' contract path and contract-event version 3, which archives their events (4c-2b; Relayed ballots, Close statements), and the window check on ordinary ballots (Calls) and the contracts themselves (4c-3). [[P4-3]] part 2c-2 brings the availability call, through which the trustee seats attest that the board was available to them ([[availability]], Contract path), and contract-event version 8, which archives their events (Availability records; Contract-event record, Event tables (version 8)). [[P2-17]] part 2b-2b specifies the rolling call, through which a rolling poll's ballot carries the root its proof was made against, and contract-event version 10, which archives its event (Rolling ballots; Contract-event record, Event tables (version 10)), part 2b-2c-1 a rolling poll's delivery records, its postings through a call of their own that carries the root the same way (Delivery records (ballot version 5)), and part 2b-2c-2 the relayed rolling call, through which a rolling ballot forced through L1 carries its root, and contract-event version 11, which archives its event (Relayed rolling ballots; L1 relay, Rolling ballots; Contract-event record, Event tables (version 11)).

### Membership proofs

The board accepts a ballot only if its membership proof verifies ([[membership]], Owned elsewhere), so non-members can't post at all, and it accepts exactly the proofs [[membership]] (Proof encoding, Verification) accepts, so the contract and the verifier never disagree about one (T-31; [[verifier]], Principles). `MembershipProofLib` (`packages/contracts`) checks a proof `A ‖ B ‖ C` against signals `[ρ, ν, m̂, ŝ]` in this order, and the first failure is its verdict:

| Verdict          | When                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| ---------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `truncated`      | The proof is shorter than 256 bytes (at offset 0)                                                                                                                                                                    |
| `trailing-bytes` | It is longer (at offset 256)                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `non-canonical`  | At offset 0 or 192: `A` or `C` has a coordinate at or above `p`, is the identity (both coordinates 0), or isn't on `y² = x³ + 3`. At offset 64: `B` has a coordinate at or above `p` or is the identity (all four 0) |
| `signal`         | A signal is at or above `r`. Signals are never reduced                                                                                                                                                               |
| `root`           | `ρ = 0`, which any secret can prove membership of ([[membership]], Statement)                                                                                                                                        |
| `non-canonical`  | At offset 64: `B` isn't on the twist or isn't in the order-`r` subgroup, which only `ecPairing` checks (below), so it is reported after `signal` and `root`                                                          |
| `pairing`        | Groth16's equation fails                                                                                                                                                                                             |

The equation is [[membership]]'s, under the pinned depth-32 key, which the contract holds as constants (the 768-byte `vk` of [[membership]], Verification key; a test compares it and its hash with `vectors/membership-proof.json`), never an input, a depth or another contract's key. It computes `L = IC_0 + ρ·IC_1 + ν·IC_2 + m̂·IC_3 + ŝ·IC_4` with the `ecMul` and `ecAdd` precompiles (EIP-196) and checks `e(−A, B) · e(α, β) · e(L, γ) · e(C, δ) = 1` with one `ecPairing` call of four pairs (EIP-197), in the precompiles' encoding, which is the proof's. `ecPairing` fails on a G2 point that isn't on the twist or isn't in the order-`r` subgroup; every other input is a valid point by then, so a failed call is `B`'s `non-canonical`. A failed precompile call consumes all the gas it was given, so the pairing is given exactly 600,000 gas, over three times what four pairs cost under EIP-1108 (181,000), and the check reverts unless that much is available (with the 1/64 a call keeps back, EIP-150): a call that ran out of gas never reads as a broken `B`. The whole check costs about 200,000 gas, but needs about 620,000 available to run; every decode failure except `B`'s twist and subgroup check is found before any precompile runs. The cap is a trade-off: a successful call consumes only the pairing's price, so a larger cap costs nothing but gas limit, which a forced deposit buys on L1 ([[0004-l2-choice]], Forced-deposit limits), and a smaller one sooner fails every proof after a repricing (Known-weak).

The signals come from the definition the registry recorded and from the ballot itself, never from the submitter, except the root a rolling poll's ballot, relayed or not, or a rolling posting's ballot, supplies (Rolling ballots; Relayed rolling ballots; Delivery records (ballot version 5)): `ρ` is the tier's (or cell's) root, `ŝ = F(S)` with `S = H(DS("abolish/v1/nullifier-scope", definition_hash ‖ u8(tier) ‖ encode(root)))` computed on chain, refusing a tier other than 0, 1 or 2, a root of 0 and a root at or above `r`, in that order ([[membership]], Scope), and `m̂ = F(M)` with `M` from [[revoting]] (Message), `F(x) = int(keccak256(x)) >> 8` ([[membership]], Field mapping). Proofs are malleable, so nothing compares proof bytes to detect a repeat (T-38).

### Ballots

The board accepts a ballot record (`0x0002`) only if it passes every admission check of [[ballot-validity]] that needs no ristretto255 arithmetic ([[0028-board-contract-checks]], 3B), so a non-member can't post, and a member's record carries chosen bytes only where the contract can't look (T-25, T-17, T-52). `BallotCheckLib.check(record, registry, qualified)` (`packages/contracts`) takes the record, the election registry, which the board pins, and the registered key set's seats; it reads the election's parameters from the registry by the record's `definition_hash` (Election registry, Parameters) and nothing about the election from the submitter. It runs these steps in order and reverts on the first failure, with a decode error and its offset ([[notation]]) or an admission code:

1. **Header.** A record shorter than its 2-byte type is `truncated` at 0, and one ending after a valid type is `truncated` at 2. A record type other than `0x0002` is `unexpected-record-type` if [[versioning]] lists it (`0x0001` to `0x001a` when the board is built, which it can't learn past), and `unknown-record-type` otherwise, both at 0.
2. **The election.** `definition_hash` at offset 3 (`truncated` if the record ends first). If the registry recorded no parameters for it (`ballot_version` 0), `ballot-stray`: the election is found first, since the version a record must have is the one its election pins.
3. **The version.** A version other than the one the election pins is `profile-mismatch` at 2, checked before whether the version is known, as [[notation]] (Strict decoding) orders it; a pinned version other than 1, 2 or 3 is `unknown-version` at 2, so a profile the board can't read admits no ballot. Ballots (version 4), below, adds version 4.
4. **The fixed fields.** Version 1's `tier` above 2 is `invalid-enum` at 35 (versions 2 and 3 take any `u8` as `cell`); `nullifier` at or above `r` is `non-canonical` at 36; a record ending before the committed vote is `truncated` at the field it ends in, the proof's at 108. The proof's points are decoded in step 9.
5. **The committed vote**, framed strictly at 364 under the record's version ([[ballot-validity]], Committed vote; Committed vote (version 3)), left to right: each count above its maximum is `length-over-max` at the count, before anything it counts is read (T-52); each `element` is `non-canonical` at its offset unless its 32 bytes, read little-endian, are a value `s` with `0 < s < 2²⁵⁵ − 19` and `s` even, which is RFC 9496 §4.3.1's canonical and non-negative checks and the identity's refusal; each `scalar` is `non-canonical` unless below `ℓ`; a record ending inside a field is `truncated` at that field; bytes after the last field are `trailing-bytes`. Whether an element decodes to a point (a square root in that field, then the sign of `x·y` and `y ≠ 0`) is the verifier's (Residuals below).
6. **`ballot-tier` or `ballot-cell`.** Version 1 names a tier: it must be the tier of exactly one cell of the registry's `cellTiers`, which is that cell; a tier no cell has, or several have, is `ballot-tier`. A definition that splits a tier into cells pins ballot version 2 or 3 by [[verifier]] 2.3's `profile-combination`, which the registry refuses otherwise (Election registry, Registration, rule 9), so refusing such a ballot refuses nothing honest. Versions 2 and 3 name a cell, `ballot-cell` unless it is below `cell_count`. The cell's tier and root come from `cell(definition_hash, c)`.
7. **`validity-shape`** against the parameters: `T` options of exactly `k` commitments each, `n` share hashes, `T` option proofs, and, under ballot versions 1 and 2, one sum proof of `max − min + 1` branches for the one question (parameters with several questions fit no version-1 or version-2 shape), or under version 3 one range proof per question with its question's `max_q − min_q + 1` branches.
8. **`share-hash-unqualified`**: a seat from 1 to `n` outside `qualified` whose share hash isn't 32 zero bytes. `qualified` is the seats of the registered key set the trustee registry records for the election's stub and panel (Trustee registry, Registration; [[0028-board-contract-checks]], 2B), as `keySetOf` returns them; the library takes it as an input, which the board reads from the trustee registry (Calls).
9. **`membership-proof`**: `ballot_hash` from the record ([[ballot-validity]], Ballot hash; Hashes (version 3)), `M` from it, the definition hash, the cell's tier and the record's L1 reference ([[revoting]], Message), and Membership proofs above with `[ρ, ν, m̂, ŝ]` the cell's root, the record's `nullifier`, `F(M)` and the scope of the cell's tier and root. A proof whose `A`, `B` or `C` is broken is `non-canonical` at 108, 172 or 300 (a `B` off the twist or outside its subgroup is found only by the pairing); any other failure, and a cell root of 0, which anyone can prove membership of, is `membership-proof`.

An admitted ballot gives its `definition_hash`, version, cell, tier, `nullifier`, `ballot_hash` and `M`, which the board's event uses (Events).

**What it leaves out.** The validity proofs (`option-proof`, `sum-proof`) need ristretto255 arithmetic and are checked by the verifier alone; so is the rest of an element's decoding (step 5) and the L1 reference ([[revoting]], Owned elsewhere), which a contract on L2 can't check. A record that passes here and fails any of them is boarded as a rejected entry ([[verifier]], Principles): it counts nothing and fails no election (T-53). So the board refuses only records the verifier rejects, never an honest ballot (T-25), and its order differs from [[ballot-validity]]'s only in finding the election before the version and the proof's points last, which changes no verdict, since the board refuses on any failure.

**Gas.** Checking a ballot of three options at `k = 2` and `n = 3` costs about 272,000 gas, most of it the membership check; framing grows with the committed vote, about 400,000 gas for the 16.8 KB vote of 32 two-option questions at `k = 2` (about 700 gas per element or scalar framed, so about a million at `MAX_OPTIONS` and `MAX_TRUSTEES`, about 1,400 of them), plus the record's calldata.

### Ballots (version 4)

Specified in [[P2-16]] part 5a and built in part 5c-2, before any registry registers a definition pinning ballot version 4 ([[delivery]], Version 2; [[ballot-validity]], Residuals (version 4); T-25, T-54, T-17). Rule 9 registers elections that pin it since part 5e-5b, once the version-2 delivery records have their contract path too (Delivery records (version 2)), sealed, and with an author panel since part 5e-5d; the tests reach the check through stubs of the registries.

The board checks a ballot record of version 4 ([[ballot-validity]], Ballot record (version 4)) with the steps of Ballots, in the same order, reading both halves. `BallotCheckLib.check` takes a second set, `authorQualified`, the seats of the author key set the election registry recorded with the definition (`authorKeySetOf(definition_hash)`, Election registry, Author panel registration), empty for a poll with no author panel; the board reads both sets before the check, never from a submitter. What changes:

- **Step 3.** A pinned version other than 1 to 4 is `unknown-version` at 2.
- **Step 4.** As version 3: `cell` is any `u8`, `nullifier` below `r`, the proof at 108.
- **Step 5.** The committed vote is framed strictly at 364 under Committed vote (version 4), its six fields in order: `vss`, `share_hashes`, `author_vss`, `author_share_hashes`, `option_proofs`, `sum_proofs`. `author_vss` is framed as `vss` (each count against its maximum before what it counts, each `element` as step 5 frames one, so none is the identity) and `author_share_hashes` as `share_hashes`.
- **Step 7, `validity-shape`**, as Checks (version 4) states it, against the parameters: `vss` has `T` entries of exactly `k` (`threshold`) elements, `share_hashes` has `n` (`panel_size`) entries; with an author panel (`author_threshold` not 0), `author_vss` has `T` entries of exactly `k_A` (`author_threshold`) elements and `author_share_hashes` has `n_A` (`author_panel_size`) entries, and without one both are empty lists, never `T` empty lists, so a ballot of a poll without one has one encoding ([[ballot-validity]], Committed vote (version 4), Notes); `option_proofs` has `T` entries, and `sum_proofs` one range proof per question with its question's `max_q − min_q + 1` branches, as under version 3. Ballot versions 1 to 3 have no author half, so a definition with an author panel fits none of their shapes; the registry pins version 4 for every such definition, so this refuses nothing honest and keeps step 8 from reading an author list a record doesn't carry.
- **Step 8, `share-hash-unqualified`**: a default seat from 1 to `n` outside `qualified` whose `share_hashes` entry isn't 32 zero bytes, then an author seat from 1 to `n_A` outside `authorQualified` whose `author_share_hashes` entry isn't, each panel in seat order, the default panel first, as [[ballot-validity]] (Admission (version 4)) reports them. A ballot whose author half names a seat with no registered keys would let its share hash carry 32 chosen bytes the verifier then rejects only after it is archived; checking it here keeps them off the board (T-17, T-52).
- **Step 9.** `ballot_hash` is Hashes (version 4)'s, over the committed vote as encoded, and `M` version 2's, as for versions 2 and 3.

An admitted version-4 ballot gives what a version-3 one gives (its `definition_hash`, version, cell, tier, `nullifier`, `ballot_hash` and `M`). The computed vote commitment `C_j = E^D_{j,0} · E^A_{j,0}` and the validity proofs over it need ristretto255 arithmetic and are left to the verifier with the rest of What it leaves out: the board neither multiplies the halves nor checks that any `C_j` is well-formed. So the board still refuses only records the verifier rejects (T-25).

**Gas.** The author half adds its framing, about 700 gas per element as for the default half, and its share hashes' comparison with `authorQualified`: about 0.7 KB of calldata and 14,000 gas of framing at 10 options with `k_A = 2` and `n_A = 3`, and at `MAX_OPTIONS` and an author panel of `MAX_TRUSTEES` about 33 KB of calldata and on the order of a million gas more, which a forced deposit still carries ([[0004-l2-choice]], Forced-deposit limits; [[ballot-validity]], Residuals (version 4), Larger ballots).

### Rolling ballots

Specified in [[P2-17]] part 2b-2b and built in its part 5, before any registry registers a definition pinning ballot version 5: rule 9 refuses every one until then (Election registry, Registration). The board's call is built since part 5d-1 and the relay's rolling call since part 5d-2a; the board's relayed rolling call, the rolling posting call and the registry that serves ballot version 5 follow in later parts of part 5. A rolling poll's ballots prove membership against the root of the step they name, which the verifier computes from the group registry's events ([[membership]], Root clock and steps), and no such root is registered anywhere: the group registry keeps no tree and the election registry records each rolling cell with the root 0 (Election registry, Parameters). So a rolling poll's ballot reaches the board by its own call, `submitRollingBallot(bytes32 root, bytes record)`, which takes the root the submitter proved against beside the record, not in it ([[0028-rolling-electorates]], Direct submission; [[membership]], Owned elsewhere; T-25). The call reverts on the first of these, in order:

1. **Calldata**: canonical, as every call's (Calls): the selector, the root's word, the offset `0x40`, the record's length, the record, zero padding and nothing after.
2. **The ballot**: the record passes Ballots, with `qualified` and `authorQualified` read as `submitBallot` reads them and the changes below, and with its errors.
3. **`ballot-window`**: as `submitBallot`'s (Calls), from the block's timestamp, after the ballot check.

Then it emits `RollingBallotSubmitted` (Events), carrying the root and the record, and returns. What changes in the ballot check, which reads version 5 on this call alone:

- **Step 3.** As before, a record version other than the one the election pins is `profile-mismatch` at 2, checked first; then the election must pin ballot version 5: a pinned version other than 5 is `unknown-version` at 2 here, since a fixed poll's root is registered and must never be replaced by a submitter's. The other ballot paths read versions 1 to 4 only (Ballots (version 4), step 3), so a rolling poll's ballot is `unknown-version` at 2 there, and this call and the relayed rolling call (Relayed rolling ballots) are its contract paths.
- **Step 4.** As version 4's: `cell` any `u8`, `nullifier` below `r`, the proof at 108; then the `step` at 364, any `u32`, which the contract never compares with the root clock (a record ending inside it is `truncated` at 364).
- **Step 5.** The committed vote is framed at 368 as Ballots (version 4) frames it, its six fields in order, with nothing after it.
- **Step 6, `ballot-cell`**, as for versions 2 to 4: `cell` below `cell_count`, its tier from `cell(definition_hash, c)`, whose recorded root the call never reads.
- **Steps 7 and 8** as version 4's.
- **Step 9, `membership-proof`**: `ballot_hash` is Hashes (version 5)'s, which binds the step, `M` version 2's over it ([[revoting]], Message), and Membership proofs runs with `[ρ, ν, m̂, ŝ]` the supplied root, the record's `nullifier`, `F(M)` and `ŝ_t = F(S_t)` with `S_t = H(DS("abolish/v1/rolling-nullifier-scope", definition_hash ‖ u8(t)))`, `t` the cell's tier, computed on chain ([[membership]], Rolling scope), never the fixed scope, which would name a root. A supplied root of 0, which any secret can prove membership of, or at or above `r` is `membership-proof`, as a registered root of 0 is on the other paths.

An admitted ballot gives what a version-4 one gives and its supplied root, which the event carries. The contract can't compute step roots (it has no tree, and the root clock's steps read the chain derived from L1, [[membership]], Root clock and steps), so it checks the proof against whatever root it is given, and the root is never trusted: [[verifier]] 5.1 rejects a ballot whose supplied root isn't the root of the step it names (`root-step-mismatch`, [[ballot-validity]], Admission (version 5)), and the board still carries it as a rejected entry that counts nothing and fails no election (T-53). So the board refuses only records the verifier rejects, and the verifier never takes a root from a submitter (T-04, T-08).

**One root per record.** The proof fixes the root: given `A`, `B`, `C`, Groth16's equation fixes `e(L, γ)`, and `e(·, γ)` is injective on G1 since G1 has prime order and the pinned key's `γ` isn't the identity, so it fixes `L`; `L` is affine in `ρ` with the pinned key's `IC_1`, a point of order `r`, the other signals come from the record and the registry, and both roots are below `r`, so at most one `ρ` passes. So the call keeps Calls' Repeats, by the record's bytes alone: a resubmission of an accepted record is the no-op whatever root comes with it, since the record's own root is already in the event, and a copy of an honest record with another root fails `membership-proof` and isn't remembered, so a front-runner can neither keep the honest submission off the board nor attach another root to it (T-25, T-38). A copy whose proof was re-randomised is another record, accepted as such, under the root its proof fixes: the verifier finds it a repeat by `M` (5.7).

**What it leaves to others.** `submitRelayedBallot` reads versions 1 to 4 only; a rolling poll's ballot forced through L1 comes by the relay's rolling call to `submitRelayedRollingBallot`, which checks it as this call does against the root the relay carried (Relayed rolling ballots). A posting's ballot is checked by its own call, which takes a supplied root as this one does (Delivery records (ballot version 5)).

**What a submitter controls.** In a fixed poll only a member can post; in a rolling poll anyone can build a tree containing a secret of their own, prove against its root and post a ballot the board accepts and the verifier rejects, at their own gas: spam control is gas only ([[0028-rolling-electorates]], Direct submission; T-52). So in a rolling poll the chosen-bytes channel that was a member's alone is open to anyone, at gas cost: the L1 reference, every validity-proof scalar, every VSS element (any even value below `2²⁵⁵ − 19`) and the step of a ballot the verifier rejects, up to the most a version-5 record frames, archived for good (Known-weak, What a member's direct submission can still carry; T-17). The supplied root adds only a ground Poseidon output of a tree its submitter builds, never 254 chosen bits. The rolling posting call opens "A member can post chosen ciphertexts" to anyone in the same way, which is recorded there (Delivery records (ballot version 5), What a sender controls; Known-weak).

**Gas.** The rolling scope's two hashes replace the fixed scope's, and the root's word adds 32 bytes of calldata, so a rolling ballot costs about what a version-4 ballot of the same shape does.

### Deployment

The board pins the election registry and the trustee registry at construction, as immutables, and reverts its deployment unless both addresses are non-zero, distinct and not its own. It also pins `relay`, the L1 relay's address (L1 relay), non-zero, whose alias alone may call its relayed-ballot call (Relayed ballots), and reverts its deployment if it is zero; [[verifier]] 1.2 masks it too and compares it with the definition's `l1_relay`. It reads every election's parameters, ceremony and key set from these two alone, never from a caller ([[0028-board-contract-checks]], 1C and 2B). The registry pins the board in turn (Election registry, Deployment), so the three addresses are fixed before any is deployed, from the deployer's nonces or by CREATE2 ([[0028-board-contract-checks]], Consequences). The two addresses are immutables, which [[verifier]] 1.2 MUST mask and compare with the definition's `election_registry` and `trustee_registry`, as it does the group registry's immutables, so a board pinning other registries never verifies (T-64); the definition's `chain` rule names all three. It has no admin, owner, pause or upgrade path, and holds no funds.

**The ballot check.** The board runs Ballots (and a posting's ballot check, Delivery records) in a second contract, `BallotCheck`, which its constructor creates (`new BallotCheck()`, the board's first creation, so at the address `CREATE` gives the board and nonce 1) and pins as the immutable `ballotCheck`; every ballot path calls its function `check(record, registry, qualified, authorQualified)`, and the rolling call (Rolling ballots) its `checkRolling(record, registry, root, qualified, authorQualified)`, as a static call, passing the election registry the board pins and the key sets it read, and takes its result or its revert unchanged, so a refusal is the same error as when the check ran inside the board. `BallotCheck` has no storage, no immutables, no constructor arguments and no call that changes state, and is a view of what it is given: anyone may call it, which tells them only what the board would decide. It exists for EIP-170: with the availability call the board's runtime code was 24,484 bytes of 24,576, and ballot version 4's framing would not fit (Known-weak). A contract the board creates, rather than a linked library or an address passed at construction, keeps the board's constructor and deployment as they were and lets no deployer pair a board with another check: [[verifier]] 1.2 MUST mask `ballotCheck`, require the masked word to be the `CREATE` address of the board and nonce 1, and match that address's runtime code against the release build of `BallotCheck`, as it matches the board's (T-64). The call costs the board a static call and a copy of the record into memory, about 3 gas per byte plus memory expansion (about 100,000 more gas at the largest version-4 record's 80 KB), and keeps back 1/64 of the gas it forwards, so a ballot's check needs about 630,000 gas available rather than 620,000 (Membership proofs).

### Delivery records

The board takes a share receipt (`0x000c`), share posting (`0x000d`), share complaint (`0x000e`), share-receipt batch (`0x0016`), share-complaint batch (`0x0017`) or obligation root (`0x001a`) for any election its registry registered, as [[delivery]] (Direct submit) requires, checking only what needs no ML-KEM or ristretto255 arithmetic ([[0028-board-contract-checks]], 3B). `DeliveryCheckLib` (`packages/contracts`) runs these steps in order and reverts on the first failure, naming the record's position in its call, with a decode error and its offset ([[notation]]) or one of the codes below:

1. **Header.** As the ballot check's step 1, with the call's type in place of `0x0002`: a record of another type that [[versioning]] lists is `unexpected-record-type`, any other `unknown-record-type`, both at 0.
2. **The election.** `definition_hash` at offset 3. If the registry recorded no parameters for it (`ballot_version` 0), `delivery-stray`.
3. **The version.** Ballot versions 1 to 3 fix every delivery type at version 1 ([[delivery]], Versions). If the election pins a ballot version above 3, which the board can't read, the record is `unknown-version` at 2; otherwise a version other than 1 is `profile-mismatch` at 2. Delivery records (version 2), below, adds ballot version 4's records.
4. **The fields**, framed strictly ([[delivery]], Records): a record ending inside a field is `truncated` at that field, and bytes after the last are `trailing-bytes`. Each `element` (a complaint's `D`, a posting's `ct_dh`) is framed as the ballot check's step 5 frames one, each `scalar` (a complaint's `c` and `z`, a posting's `pok_c` and `pok_z`) is below `ℓ`, each `p256-sig` has `1 ≤ r < n` and `1 ≤ s ≤ (n − 1) / 2` ([[trustee-identity]], Signatures), else `non-canonical` at the field; a batch's count above its maximum (1,024 pairs, 512 complaints) is `length-over-max` at its count (offset 36), before any entry is read, a count of 0 `non-canonical` there, and an entry whose `ballot_hash` isn't above the one before `non-canonical` at that `ballot_hash`, a complaint entry's `D`, `c` and `z` framed as a complaint's ([[delivery]], Share-receipt batch, Share-complaint batch); a posting's `sealed` longer than 4,116 bytes is `length-over-max` at its count, before anything it counts is read (T-52), and one whose bytes end early is `truncated` after its count, where its bytes start, as `packages/crypto` reads every `bytes<M>`. An obligation root is decoded as [[delivery]] (Obligation root, Decoding) decodes it: a `kind` other than 1 or 2 is `invalid-enum` at 36, a `count` of 0 `non-canonical` at 37, every field is present (else `truncated` at the first one missing) before a `leaves_cid` whose first four bytes aren't the ones its kind and count give is `non-canonical` at 73, and the signature is framed after it; nothing reads the leaves.
5. **`delivery-seat`**: `seat` is a seat of the registered key set that `keySetOf(stub_hash, panel_id)` returns for the election's `ceremony(definition_hash)` (Election registry, Parameters). Seat 0 fails whatever the set says, and so does any seat outside it.
6. **`delivery-signature`** (receipts, batches, complaints and roots): the signature verifies under the seat's registered per-election signing key, `signingKeyOf(stub_hash, panel_id, seat)`, never its identity key ([[trustee-identity]], Which key signs what), over `m` under `abolish/v1/sig/share-receipt` (the 97 bytes before the signature), `abolish/v1/sig/share-receipt-batch` (the `37 + 64·c` bytes before it), `abolish/v1/sig/share-complaint` (the 161 bytes before it), `abolish/v1/sig/share-complaint-batch` (the `37 + 128·c` bytes before it) or `abolish/v1/sig/obligation-root` (the 106 bytes before it), once per record, with the digest computed on chain as [[trustee-identity]] (On chain) requires.
7. **A posting's ballot.** `posting-length`: `sealed` is `4 + 64 · T + 16` bytes for the election's `option_count` `T` ([[share-encryption]], Ciphertext), so its length is fixed. Then the ballot record the call carries passes the whole ballot check (Ballots), membership proof included, with `qualified` the key set of the election it names, with that check's errors; `posting-ballot`: its `definition_hash` is the posting's, and its `ballot_hash` (Ballots, step 9) is the posting's (the ballot hash binds the ballot's definition, never the posting's own field, so both are compared, or the seat, key set and length above would be judged against another election than the ballot's); `posting-hash`: `H(DS("abolish/v1/share-ciphertext", ct))` over the posting's `ciphertext` as encoded equals the ballot's `share_hashes[seat]`, which exists, since the ballot passed `validity-shape` and the seat is a key-set seat (a seat beyond the ballot's share count fails all the same).
8. **`root-repeat`** (roots only; the board's, not `DeliveryCheckLib`'s): the board holds no root of the record's `seat` and `kind` for its `definition_hash`. The first root of each seat and kind that passes the steps above fills that slot, and only a byte-identical repeat of it is accepted after (the no-op of Calls, Repeats), so no seat commits two receipt roots or two complaint roots, and since only a root whose signature verifies reaches this step, no one but the seat can fill its slot ([[delivery]], Obligation root, One per seat and kind; T-17, T-52). A refused root fills nothing. The slot keeps the accepted root's record hash, `H(DS("abolish/v1/obligation-root", record))` over its whole encoding ([[delivery]], Leaf availability), computed on chain with SHA-256, which `availability-root` reads (Availability records).

So only a registered seat can put a receipt or complaint on the contract path, and a posting carries only `m`, which the board can't check, and the ciphertext that a ballot passing the ballot check committed to, so only a member can choose a posting's ciphertext. What each can still choose is listed under Known-weak (T-17). What the board leaves out is everything [[delivery]] says it can't check: whether a receipt's `share_hash` is the ballot's, whether a posting passes the public check, whether a complaint is proven, and whether a record is in time. The delivery check reads every accepted record as it reads the record itself ([[delivery]], Delivery check), so one that fails it is ignored, and none ever blocks a passing one: the board accepts any number per seat and ballot (Calls). A batch's `seat` is at offset 35, not 67 as in the other records, so the seat step reads it there; nothing else of a pair is checked: its ballot hash and share hash are free to the seat, as a single receipt's are (Known-weak).

A posting's ballot is checked, never admitted: it isn't emitted, and it counts only if its own ballot entry or direct submission is admitted ([[delivery]], Direct submit). It passes the whole ballot check because a ballot only framed would let anyone make one up, with no options and one share hash of a ciphertext of their choosing, and archive that ciphertext, over a kilobyte of chosen bytes per posting, as a posting (T-17, T-52). Every ballot a client casts passes the check, whatever path it took, since the check refuses only what the verifier rejects (Ballots), so no honest posting is refused (T-25). The call carries the whole ballot record, so a client posts with the record it cast; nothing of the ballot reaches the archive. The check costs the poster about 250,000 more gas, mostly the membership proof.

### Delivery records (version 2)

Specified in [[P2-16]] parts 5a and 5b; the share receipt, posting and complaint are built in part 5c-3, and both batches and the obligation root in part 5c-4. Ballot version 4 fixes every delivery type at version 2, which names the seat's panel ([[delivery]], Version 2): the share receipt, posting and complaint since part 5a, and both batches and the obligation root since part 5b. `DeliveryCheckLib` checks version-2 records with the steps of Delivery records, in the same order, with these changes:

- **Step 3.** If the election pins ballot version 4, a record of any of the six types of a version other than 2 is `profile-mismatch` at 2. If it pins ballot versions 1 to 3, every type is still fixed at version 1, so a version-2 record is `profile-mismatch`. A pinned ballot version above 4 is `unknown-version` at 2; Delivery records (ballot version 5), below, adds ballot version 5.
- **Step 4.** The fields are framed as version 1's with `panel`, an `enum8<1, 2>`, at offset 67 between `ballot_hash` and `seat`: any other value is `invalid-enum` at 67 ([[delivery]], Records (version 2), Decoding), and every later field is one byte further on, `seat` at 68. A share receipt is 165 bytes, a complaint 229, and a posting `1,309 + 64 · T`. In both batches and the obligation root, which have no `ballot_hash` before their seat, `panel` is at offset 35 (`invalid-enum` there) and `seat` at 36: a batch's count is at 37, with `length-over-max` and `non-canonical` there as under version 1, and its first entry at 41; a root's `kind` is at 37, its `count` at 38, its `leaves_cid` at 74 and its signature at 110, each checked as under version 1 ([[delivery]], Records (version 2), Decoding). A batch of `c` entries is `105 + 64·c` or `105 + 128·c` bytes, and a root 174.
- **Step 5, `delivery-seat`**: `seat` is a seat of the panel key set of the panel the record names: for panel 1, the registered key set `keySetOf(stub_hash, panel_id)` returns for `ceremony(definition_hash)`, as under version 1; for panel 2, the author key set `authorKeySetOf(definition_hash)` returns (Election registry, Author panel registration). Seat 0 fails whatever the set says, and so does any seat outside it; a record naming panel 2 in a poll with no author panel fails here, since that set is empty ([[delivery]], Records (version 2), Decoding).
- **Step 6, `delivery-signature`**: the signature verifies under the seat's signing key in its panel key set, `signingKeyOf(stub_hash, panel_id, seat)` for panel 1 and `authorSigningKeyOf(definition_hash, seat)` for panel 2, over `m` under `abolish/v1/sig/share-receipt-2` (the 98 bytes before the signature), `abolish/v1/sig/share-receipt-batch-2` (the `38 + 64·c` bytes before it), `abolish/v1/sig/share-complaint-2` (the 162 bytes before it), `abolish/v1/sig/share-complaint-batch-2` (the `38 + 128·c` bytes before it) or `abolish/v1/sig/obligation-root-2` (the 107 bytes before it) ([[delivery]], Records (version 2); [[share-encryption]], Share receipt (ballot version 4)). The tags are new, so no version-1 signature verifies on a version-2 record or the reverse (T-31).
- **Step 7, a posting's ballot**: `posting-length` as under version 1, `sealed` being `4 + 64 · T + 16` bytes. The ballot record the call carries passes the whole ballot check of Ballots (version 4), with both sets of the election it names; `posting-ballot` as under version 1; `posting-hash`: `H(DS("abolish/v1/share-ciphertext", ct))` over the posting's `ciphertext` equals the ballot's entry for the posting's seat in its panel's list, `share_hashes[seat]` for panel 1 and `author_share_hashes[seat]` for panel 2, which exists, since the ballot passed `validity-shape` and the seat is in its panel's key set.
- **Step 8, `root-repeat`**: the slot is kept per `definition_hash`, panel, seat and kind, so seat `i` of the author panel and seat `i` of the default panel each fill their own, and a version-1 root, which only ballot versions 1 to 3 fix, fills the default panel's ([[delivery]], Records (version 2), One per panel, seat and kind). The slot keeps the accepted root's record hash, as under version 1.

So an author seat has the contract path [[delivery]] (Version 2, Boarding, direct submit and client rules) requires: its receipts and complaints are accepted under its registered signing key, and a voter's posting to it is checked against the author half's share hashes, exactly as a default seat's are against the default half's (T-25, T-54). A record for one panel is checked only against that panel's key set and list, so it never stands for a seat of the other (T-37, T-29). The repeats rule is unchanged: a version-2 record is remembered by the keccak-256 of its bytes, which begin with its type and version, so no version-1 record is its repeat.

Version-2 batches and roots name their panel, so an author seat forces what it owes through L1 with the same batch and root calls as a default seat, one signature verification per batch or root, and an election pinning ballot version 4 can commit roots ([[delivery]], Capacity), each judged available by its own panel's attestations ([[delivery]], Leaf availability (version 2)). The board takes them since [[P2-16]] part 5c-4: `availability-root` looks an attestation's roots up in its own panel's slots only (Availability records), and the election registry serves definitions pinning ballot version 4 since part 5e-5b, and a sealed author panel since part 5e-5d (Election registry, Registration, rule 9). Contract-event version 9 archives every record of this section and of Ballots (version 4) under the events version 8 names, its framing reading both (Contract-event record, What the board frames (version 9)).

### Delivery records (ballot version 5)

Specified in [[P2-17]] part 2b-2c-1 and built in its part 5, with the rolling call, before any registry registers a definition pinning ballot version 5: rule 9 refuses every one until then (Election registry, Registration). Ballot version 5 keeps version 4's delivery records unchanged: every one of the six types at version 2, naming the seat's panel, with version 4's ciphertexts, signatures and tags ([[delivery]], Versions; [[share-encryption]], Ballot version 5; [[ballot-validity]], Shares, delivery and openings (version 5)). So `DeliveryCheckLib` checks a rolling poll's records with the steps of Delivery records (version 2), in the same order, with these changes:

- **Step 3.** If the election pins ballot version 4 or 5, a record of any of the six types of a version other than 2 is `profile-mismatch` at 2; a pinned ballot version above 5 is `unknown-version` at 2.
- **Step 7, a posting's ballot.** A rolling poll's ballot proves membership against the root of the step it names, which no contract can compute and no registry records (Rolling ballots), so it is checked against a root its sender supplies, as the rolling call checks one. `submitPosting` takes no root, and its ballot check reads versions 1 to 4 only, so a version-5 ballot is `unknown-version` at 2 there; a rolling poll's posting comes by its own call (Rolling postings, below).

Receipts, complaints, both batches and the obligation root carry no ballot, so they keep their calls (Calls), checked as under ballot version 4: a seat of either panel receipts, complains and forces what it owes in a rolling poll exactly as in a fixed one (T-25, T-54). What a node owes under a rolling poll counts each rolling tier's members over the poll ([[delivery]], What a node owes), so the forced path's capacity must be sized for that growth before any registry accepts a rolling definition ([[delivery]], Capacity).

**Rolling postings.** `submitRollingPosting(bytes32 root, bytes posting, bytes ballot)` takes a share posting, the version-5 ballot record it delivers and the root that ballot's membership proof was made against. It reverts on the first of these, in order:

1. **Calldata**: canonical, as every call's (Calls): the selector, the root's word, the posting's offset `0x60`, the ballot's offset `0x80 + 32·⌈p / 32⌉` for a posting of `p` bytes, the posting's length, the posting, zero padding, the ballot's length, the ballot, zero padding, and nothing after.
2. **The posting**: steps 1 to 6 of Delivery records under the changes above, named as position 0, with their errors: under an election pinning ballot version 5 the posting is version 2, its fields framed, its seat in its own panel's key set.
3. **`posting-length`**: as under version 1, `sealed` is `4 + 64 · T + 16` bytes for the election's `option_count` `T`.
4. **The ballot**: the record passes the ballot check of Rolling ballots (its step 2), with `qualified` and `authorQualified` read for the election the ballot names, as `submitBallot` reads them, and with that check's errors. A pinned ballot version other than 5 is `unknown-version` at 2, so the call never takes a fixed poll's ballot, whose registered root a supplied one must never replace; the membership proof is checked against `root` under the rolling scope `ŝ_t`, and a `root` of 0 or at or above `r` is `membership-proof`.
5. **`posting-ballot`** and **`posting-hash`**: as under version 2, with `ballot_hash` Hashes (version 5)'s, which binds the step, and the posting's ciphertext hashed against its own panel's list.

Then it emits `SharePosted` (Events) with the posting alone and returns, as `submitPosting` does. No window applies, as to every posting (Calls, `ballot-window`). The root is neither emitted nor remembered: a posting's ballot is checked, never admitted (Delivery records), and counts only through its own ballot entry or rolling event, where the verifier compares a supplied root with the step's own (`root-step-mismatch`, [[ballot-validity]], Admission (version 5)). So the posting's event is version 2's, which contract-event versions 9 and 10 frame (Contract-event record, What the board frames (version 9)), and this call needs no contract-event version of its own.

**The root decides nothing a verifier reads.** A proof fixes its root (Rolling ballots, One root per record), so a copy of an honest call with another root fails `membership-proof` and isn't remembered; and a posting's repeat is its bytes alone (Calls, Repeats), whatever ballot and root carry it, so an accepted posting resent with another root is the no-op, and a front-runner can neither keep the voter's posting off the board nor make it carry a root of the front-runner's choosing (T-25, T-38). A root the board accepts that isn't the root of the step the ballot names (a ballot proved against another step's tree) still lets the posting through; that ballot is rejected wherever it is boarded (`root-step-mismatch`), so its postings deliver nothing ([[delivery]], Delivery check; T-53). The client posts with the root its ballot was proved against, the step root it rebuilt itself at cast ([[ballot-validity]], Casting (version 5)).

**What a sender controls** (T-17, T-52). In a fixed poll only a member can make up a ballot that passes the check and post chosen ciphertexts against it (Known-weak, A member can post chosen ciphertexts). Here, as with the rolling call (Rolling ballots, What a submitter controls), anyone can build a tree containing a secret of their own, make up a version-5 ballot that passes the check against its root, never submit it, and post a ciphertext of their choosing to each seat of both panels: up to `n + n_A` ciphertexts of about 1.2 to 5.4 KB of chosen bytes per ballot made up, each in a posting of `1,309 + 64 · T` bytes, each paying a ballot check and its own calldata. The board can't bound this without the step roots, which it can't compute, or without seeing the board; requiring the ballot first through the rolling call would archive every posting's ballot a second time and leave no contract path for a posting whose ballot went through the board path (T-25, T-54). So it is recorded, not bounded, as the rolling call's own channel is (Known-weak): the archive keeps the postings, the delivery check ignores a posting whose ballot isn't on the board ([[delivery]], Delivery check), and our posting relay, once it sends rolling postings, pays only for a posting whose ballot passes the verifier's admission, which takes no root from anyone ([[posting-relay]], Sending; Known-weak).

**Gas.** A rolling posting costs about what a version-2 posting of the same shape does under ballot version 4: the rolling scope's two hashes replace the fixed scope's, and the root's word adds 32 bytes of calldata; part 5 measures it.

### Calls

Each call takes canonical calldata only: exactly the standard ABI encoding of its `bytes` arguments, every offset the one `abi.encode` writes, zero padding and nothing after (`CanonicalCalldata`), so a transaction carries no bytes the records don't define (T-17). A call accepts every record it is given or reverts whole.

| Call                                                                                                                  | Accepts                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `submitBallot(bytes record)`                                                                                          | A ballot record that passes Ballots, with `qualified` the seats of `keySetOf(ceremony(definition_hash))` and, for version 4, `authorQualified` those of `authorKeySetOf(definition_hash)`, read before the check (zero for a record too short to name an election, which its header refuses first), in a block whose timestamp is inside the election's window (`ballot-window`, below).                                                                                                                                    |
| `submitReceipts(bytes receipts)`                                                                                      | One or more share receipts, concatenated, read in turn: each is 165 bytes if its version byte (its offset 2) is 2 and 164 otherwise (version 1, or a version its check refuses), and the argument must hold at least one and end exactly where its last record does: a remainder shorter than 3 bytes, or shorter than the length its version byte gives, is `ReceiptBatchLength`, before any record is checked; each passes Delivery records (version 2 under Delivery records (version 2)), named by its position from 0. |
| `submitReceiptBatch(bytes record)`                                                                                    | One share-receipt batch that passes Delivery records, named as position 0: one signature verification, one storage key and one event for up to 1,024 receipts, the call a trustee node forces through L1 ([[delivery]], Capacity).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `submitComplaintBatch(bytes record)`                                                                                  | One share-complaint batch that passes Delivery records, named as position 0: one signature verification, one storage key and one event for up to 512 complaints ([[delivery]], Capacity).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `submitObligationRoot(bytes record)`                                                                                  | One obligation root that passes Delivery records, `root-repeat` included, named as position 0: one signature verification, two storage keys (the record's and its slot's) and one event, whatever the number of leaves it commits to ([[delivery]], Capacity).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `submitPosting(bytes posting, bytes ballot)`                                                                          | A share posting that passes Delivery records against the ballot record `ballot`, of ballot versions 1 to 4.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `submitRollingPosting(bytes32 root, bytes posting, bytes ballot)`                                                     | A share posting that passes Delivery records against the version-5 ballot record `ballot`, its membership proof checked against `root` (Delivery records (ballot version 5), Rolling postings).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `submitComplaint(bytes record)`                                                                                       | A share complaint that passes Delivery records.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `submitRelayedBallot(uint64 index, uint64 l1Block, uint64 stamp, uint64 gasLimit, bytes record)`                      | From the L1 relay's alias only, a ballot record that passes Ballots with its stamp inside the window (Relayed ballots).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `submitRollingBallot(bytes32 root, bytes record)`                                                                     | A version-5 ballot record that passes Rolling ballots, its membership proof checked against `root`, with `qualified` and `authorQualified` read as for `submitBallot`, inside the election's window (`ballot-window`).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `submitRelayedRollingBallot(uint64 index, uint64 l1Block, uint64 stamp, uint64 gasLimit, bytes32 root, bytes record)` | From the L1 relay's alias only, a version-5 ballot record that passes Rolling ballots against `root`, with its stamp inside the window (Relayed rolling ballots).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `submitCloseStatement(bytes record)`                                                                                  | A close statement that passes Close statements.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `submitAvailabilityRecord(bytes record)`                                                                              | An availability attestation or complaint that passes Availability records: one signature verification, two storage keys (the record's and its slot's) and one event, and, for an attestation naming roots, a read of the root slots of the key set's seats.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |

**Repeats.** A record byte-identical to one the board already accepted, by any call, is accepted again as a no-op: the call succeeds, checks nothing and emits nothing ([[0028-board-contract-checks]], Consequences). So a client's retry never reads as a failure, and nothing is archived twice (T-17, T-52). Any other record that passes is accepted, however many the board holds for the same seat, ballot hash or nullifier: a different encoding of the same ballot (proofs are malleable, T-38), a re-vote, a second receipt or posting for a seat, or junk signed by a registered seat. No record occupies a slot, so neither an operator nor a seat that signs junk can stop the honest record that follows ([[delivery]], Direct submit; T-25, T-54). Four calls depart from this: for the close rule, the relayed-ballot calls never consult the repeats, so every relayed deposit they accept emits (Relayed ballots, No repeats on this path; Relayed rolling ballots), and the statement call accepts at most two statements per seat and election, the second only with another claim, since only the seat's own signatures can fill its slots (Close statements); and the root call accepts one root per seat, kind and election (Delivery records, `root-repeat`), for the same reason and so that no seat can make a verifier fetch more than two leaf objects of it ([[delivery]], Obligation root); and the availability call accepts one attestation and one complaint per definition, panel, seat and point (Availability records, `availability-repeat`), for the same reason and so that no seat archives more than four of them per election. A posting's repeat is the posting's bytes alone, whatever ballot record (and, on the rolling posting call, whatever root) carries it. The board remembers each accepted record by the keccak-256 of its bytes, a storage key nothing outside the contract recomputes, never a protocol hash; records begin with their type, so no two types' records share one. A record is remembered only once it is accepted, so a repeat is always a record that passed.

**`ballot-window`.** `submitBallot` and `submitRollingBallot` refuse a ballot unless `opens_at ≤ block.timestamp ≤ closes_at`, both read from the election registry's parameters for the record's `definition_hash` (Election registry, Parameters): the ordinary path's test of [[close]] (Window), so a ballot made after close never reaches the board by this path, whatever a sequencer does with unposted blocks after `L_δ` (T-35, T-45), and the verifier, which repeats the test from the event's L2 timestamp (5.5), never sees one from an honest board (T-71). It runs after the ballot check, so a malformed or stray record after close is refused for what it is, and only once a record isn't a repeat: an accepted ballot's byte-identical resubmission after close is still the no-op above, which emits nothing. A refused ballot isn't remembered. A posting's ballot isn't admitted, so the window doesn't apply to it; postings, receipts and complaints are boarded as contract events late or not, and are in time by their submission time ([[delivery]], Windows; [[verifier]] 4.4). Who pays is never checked or recorded: a voter's client pays from a fresh, privately funded address or through a relay ([[delivery]], Direct submit; T-11, T-13).

### Relayed ballots

A ballot forced through L1 reaches the board as the deposit the L1 relay made (L1 relay, Relaying): a call from the relay's alias to `submitRelayedBallot(uint64 index, uint64 l1Block, uint64 stamp, uint64 gasLimit, bytes record)`, carrying the relay call's index `i`, its L1 block `b`, its stamp `τ` and its gas limit, and the record ([[0030-close-rule]], Forced ballots; T-50). The call reverts on the first of these, in order:

1. **`relay-sender`**: the caller is `alias(relay)` (L1 relay, Relaying), with `relay` the board's immutable. Only a deposit the relay made, or one L2 governance forges from its alias, gets past this (T-71); everything else is refused before any record is read.
2. **Calldata**: canonical, as every call's (Calls): the selector, four words each below 2⁶⁴, the offset `0xa0`, the record's length, the record, zero padding and nothing after. The relay's deposit data is exactly this (L1 relay, Relaying), so an honest deposit always passes.
3. **The ballot**: the record passes Ballots, with `qualified` and, for version 4, `authorQualified` read as `submitBallot` reads them, and with its errors. A relayed ballot is checked exactly as an ordinary one: only the path differs.
4. **`relay-window`**: `opens_at ≤ stamp ≤ closes_at`, both read from the election registry's parameters for the record's `definition_hash` (Election registry, Parameters), the test [[close]] (Window) applies to the relayed path. The stamp is the call's word, which the board can't check: a deposit forged with another stamp fails [[verifier]] 4.5, since it matches no call (L1 relay, Verification).

Then it emits `RelayedBallotSubmitted` (Events) and returns. The four call fields are carried, never judged: the board can't read L1, and the verifier matches them, with the record, against the word the relay stored for call `i` (L1 relay, Verification; `relay-unmatched`, `relay-duplicate`).

**No repeats on this path.** The call never consults the board's record of accepted bytes (Calls, Repeats): it emits for every deposit it accepts, a record byte-identical to one the board already holds included, as the relay requires (L1 relay, Relaying). Otherwise a sequencer that copied a forced ballot's bytes from L1 calldata into an ordinary block before the deposit, and withheld that block past `L_δ`, would leave the ballot with only the late ordinary copy, and the forced path's guarantee would be the ordinary path's (T-50, T-25). It still remembers the record, so a later ordinary submission of the same bytes is the no-op Repeats describes; that copy adds nothing, since the relayed one is judged by its own stamp ([[close]], Window). A relayed record is archived again only by another L1 relay call, each paid for on L1 (T-17, T-52); Known-weak lists what that leaves.

A deposit that reverts here (another sender, a record the board refuses, a stamp outside the window, too little gas) leaves the relay's call recorded on L1 with no event, which the verifier reports and never fails (L1 relay, Verification, Calls with no event). The relayed path carries ballots only: postings, receipts and complaints go through their own calls on L2 (Delivery records). This call reads ballot versions 1 to 4 only (Ballots (version 4), step 3), so a version-5 ballot deposited here is `unknown-version` and leaves its call with no event; a rolling poll's comes by the next call.

### Relayed rolling ballots

Specified in [[P2-17]] part 2b-2c-2 and built in its part 5, with the rolling call, before any registry registers a definition pinning ballot version 5: rule 9 refuses every one until then (Election registry, Registration). A rolling poll's ballot proves membership against a root no contract computes or records (Rolling ballots), so one forced through L1 must carry the root beside the record, as on the ordinary path: the relay's rolling call records the root with the call and deposits it (L1 relay, Rolling ballots), and the deposit is a call from the relay's alias to `submitRelayedRollingBallot(uint64 index, uint64 l1Block, uint64 stamp, uint64 gasLimit, bytes32 root, bytes record)` ([[0028-rolling-electorates]], Direct submission; [[0030-close-rule]], Forced ballots; T-50, T-25). It reverts on the first of these, in order:

1. **`relay-sender`**: the caller is `alias(relay)`, as for `submitRelayedBallot`, before any record is read (T-71).
2. **Calldata**: canonical, as every call's (Calls): the selector, four words each below 2⁶⁴, the root's word, the offset `0xc0`, the record's length, the record, zero padding and nothing after. The relay's rolling deposit data is exactly this (L1 relay, Rolling ballots), so an honest deposit always passes.
3. **The ballot**: the record passes the ballot check of Rolling ballots (its step 2), with `qualified` and `authorQualified` read as `submitBallot` reads them and with that check's errors: the election must pin ballot version 5 (`unknown-version` at 2 otherwise, so the call never takes a fixed poll's ballot, whose registered root a supplied one must never replace), and the membership proof is checked against `root` under the rolling scope `ŝ_t`, a `root` of 0 or at or above `r` being `membership-proof`.
4. **`relay-window`**: `opens_at ≤ stamp ≤ closes_at`, as for `submitRelayedBallot`.

Then it emits `RelayedRollingBallotSubmitted` (Events), carrying the four call fields, the root and the record, and returns. As on `submitRelayedBallot`, the call fields and the root are carried, never judged: the verifier matches all five, with the record, against the word the relay stored for call `i` (L1 relay, Verification), and compares the root with the root of the step the ballot names (`root-step-mismatch`, [[ballot-validity]], Admission (version 5)). It never consults the repeats and still remembers the record, as Relayed ballots, No repeats on this path, says of that call, for the same reason (T-50, T-25).
